The composite is drawn from the existing glyph assets, with no new asset: (a) **berthed**: the record's template glyph at the attach point plus the A17 seam quad on the shared edge; the record is **not** drawn at its authored position while berthed, and returns there on `unberth`; (b) **ABSORBED**: the same glyph at the same attach point in the HOME stroke style, with the seam replaced by the Ground erase quad; (c) the pod marker follows the drawn position of its record. **Berthed and absorbed records are excluded from the snap graph, from focus and from the pairwise `S_MIN` lint of their authored positions**, because they are not focusable; their attach rectangles are linted instead: each must not overlap any other node's bracket-padded rectangle, and HOME plus every attach rectangle must lie inside the chart frame (D.1b). The lint is re-run for each composite state of AC-CT-120: none, B berthed, B absorbed, A and B absorbed. If two records are given the same side of HOME the lint fails the build. Status is read from `web.view` on every change; a record whose status leaves ABSORBED or `berthed` (an `unberth`, or a restore) is simply drawn at its authored position again.

**Per-state paint table for the composite** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (Moment 4's only visible reward for the berth-and-weld loop, so "HOME stroke style" is defined here). Every glyph is painted in four layers, back to front: the Ground clearing (`NODE_CLEAR_PAD_PX`), the **interior fill**, the **silhouette stroke**, the **outline**; the seam and erase quads come last.

| State | Interior fill | Silhouette stroke | Outline | Last quad |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| HOME | Ground | Graphite lit, 1 px (HOME's own stroke, the reference for "HOME stroke style") | 1-px Hull Grey | the Tungsten point; no seam |
| UNSEEN | Ground | Graphite lit plain rectangle | 1-px Hull Grey | none |
| SEEN / BOARDED | Ground | Graphite lit, template silhouette | 1-px Hull Grey | the Tungsten tick if `unknown_seen` |
| `berthed` | Ground | Graphite lit, template silhouette, at the attach point | 1-px Hull Grey | solid 1-px **Hull Grey seam** quad on the shared edge (A17) |
| ABSORBED | Ground | **exactly HOME's stroke** (same colour and weight), at the attach point | 1-px Hull Grey | a **Ground erase quad** over every stroke and outline column of the shared edge (inset 1 art px at each end so the corners close); the erase repaints the **interior-fill colour (Ground)**, so the two interiors read as one and no seam is visible; no tick and no Tungsten point on the record |
| ORPHAN | Ground | Graphite mid | dashed 1-px Hull Grey | none |

The fill and the erase colour are the same Ground, which is why the erase leaves no visible seam between two fills. The art-director signs the geometry of A1 to A3 and A17 (how many stroke and outline columns the shared edge carries) **before** any golden of AC-CT-120 is captured; the erase quad spans however many columns that geometry has.
State is never carried by colour alone: every state has a distinct silhouette or mark (tick, seam, detachment, glyph), satisfying SL:1010 and HR-10 by construction. Routes, seams and rule lines are 1-art-px quads aligned to whole art px, never width-1 line draws on a fractional transform (4.7 `CanvasItem` line drawing has no antialiasing feather; -> ADR candidate (b)).
*Visibility, ruled (art-director risks R-AD-4 and R-AD-5; OQ-CT-24 closed as "add outline")* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: Graphite lit on Ground measures 2.77:1, below the 3:1 glyph floor (HR-12), and ORPHAN's Graphite mid about 1.2:1. The glyph assets (A1 to A3 and the ORPHAN variant) therefore carry a **1-px Hull Grey outline** (about 5.3:1) on every UNSEEN, SEEN, BOARDED and HOME silhouette, and ORPHAN carries a **dashed 1-px Hull Grey outline** (dashed means severed: a shape, not a colour; selectability stays absent). This is a glyph change, not a change to the WM drawing rows (WM:1041-1046). Each node also sits on a **Ground clearing** (`NODE_CLEAR_PAD_PX` 3 art px, `[PLACEHOLDER]`, a Ground quad drawn above the filament sheet and below the glyph) so a decorative filament can never out-contrast a target. The contrast of every pair is a computed palette lint (AC-CT-75c), not an eyeballed claim. The asset names are WM's A10 set: the change is routed to WM and to the art-director as a cross-GDD note.
*Source.* WM:1036-1050; ART 3.4, 4.6, 7.2, 7.4.

**CT-12. Layout data is authored, per record.**
*Rule.* A Chart-Table-owned content file (`assets/data/ui/chart_layout.json`, path provisional) holds authored node positions **per record (`derelict_id`) in chart-local art px** inside the chart safe frame (D.1b), a **`home_attach` offset per derelict record** (the attach point at which a berthed or absorbed record is drawn against HOME, CT-11 "the home-ship composite"), the depth-ring radii, the filament texture reference and a stable per-record **ordinal**; the silhouette glyph is chosen **by `template_id`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Nothing is derived from `world_slot`, leg data, ticks or distance (WM:1049, ASK-AD-7); `world_slot` is in `web.view` and is never read for layout. A missing record position is a content-lint failure; at runtime a deterministic fallback slot on the outer ring is used and a log line is emitted. Positions are authored cosmetic placements and are lint-checked so no authored distance correlates with any Web Map & Transit cost value.
*Why.* Two derelicts of one template must not overlap; a derived layout is a distance read, and a distance read is a cost number by proxy.
*Layout coordinate space (closed by checkpoint ruling Q7; OQ-CT-11 is closed).* Positions are authored in art px inside a **fixed chart frame** `CHART_FRAME` 288 x 162 art px (`[PLACEHOLDER]`, derived from the smallest spatial canvas, D.1b) and drawn at an integer **chart multiplier** `m` applied to positions only (D.1b); glyphs stay 24x24 at `H`. The **compact layout** (CT-44) applies iff `m < 1`. The ui-programmer's normalised (u, v) coordinates were declined.
*Source.* WM:1049, ASK-AD-7 (WM:1180).

**CT-13. Depth rings, filaments and the only motion.**
*Rule.* The chart field is drawn as concentric **ring bands** darkening toward the centre, ending in a centre disc that is exactly Void Steel `#0B1018`; `CHART_RING_STEPS` (3, `[PLACEHOLDER]`) counts the ring bands. All MVP nodes sit in ring 1, the outermost band, which is Ground `#1C2128` itself; rings 2 and 3 are the horizon, not content, and carry no node, label or tick (WM:1050). The darkening is an **ordering contract** (outer lightest, each step darker than the one outside it by at least `RING_STEP_MIN_CHANNEL` `[PLACEHOLDER]` per channel, centre disc darkest; ring 1 is Ground itself, the disc is Void Steel itself, and the tones between are the D.6 interpolation), not a percentage restated as a value: ART 7.4 and WM:1050 say "about 4 % darker", but the art-director found that reading cannot be satisfied together with "the inner ring reaches Void Steel" and stays near the visibility threshold (flag F-6; wording clarification routed to the art-bible owner; exact tones are derived values in D.6 and Visual/Audio, never new palette entries). `depth_label` and ring numbering carry no progress semantics (WM:1309). Filaments are Hull Grey, 1 art px, branching, non-repeating, no right angles (ART 3.4), **authored texture layers** (not procedural), drifting by whole-art-px offsets on the layer's own UI clock (CT-43) at no more than the ART 7.3 ceiling, stepped through an authored irregular interval table, floored at one second, so the step is never a metronome beat (D.5; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, G2: creative-director lean, authored irregular step table)*), **in ring 1 only**, and never under a node (every node sits on a Ground clearing, CT-11); the filaments of rings 2 and 3 are static and denser toward the centre. **Reduced motion** stops the drift and holds its current whole-pixel offset (no snap-back) and turns the bracket relocation into a cut; it does **not** turn fades into cuts (a fade is not spatial motion) *(orchestrator ruling: the art-director's reading, over the ux-designer's "fades become cuts")*. **Reduce flashing** (a separate toggle, PR-13(c)) uses the long end of the ART 7.3 fade band and forbids a hard cut of a large plate. The two toggles together are the union. Reduce flashing also lengthens the re-open interval of the governor (CT-3: 334 ms to 1000 ms), which is what reduces the flash *count*; the 100 to 120 ms fade change alone reduces the step steepness and not the count *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Everything human on the chart is static.
*Why.* The composition must pull inward (ART 2.11 carried element). `present_time` is frozen under pause (PIPE:265), so the drift cannot use it.
*Source.* ART 2.11, 3.4, 7.3, 7.4, HR-8; WM:1050; PIPE:265; PIPE PR-13(c).

**CT-14. No label of any kind beside a node or the selection.**
*Rule.* No distance, travel time, fuel figure, danger rating, loot estimate, "recommended" marker, name or number sits beside any node or the selection, **except the CT-44 neutral ordinal in the compact layout, which is not a name** (a slot mark with no identity: it never varies with status, `template_id`, kind or depth, and UNSEEN and SEEN rows render the identical key form; AC-CT-112). The only text on the chart is the state word `IN TRANSIT` (CT-15), the layer's own control hints (keycap glyphs) and, in the compact layout only, those neutral ordinals. The spatial map carries no text beside any node, UNSEEN or SEEN.
*Why.* A distance invites "closer = cheaper", a cost number by proxy (P1; WM guard rail 7; OQ-WM-34 default: "No label is required; none is derived"). The art bible's selection row mentions "label and distance beside it"; that wording is superseded by this rule and routed as a clarification to the art-bible owner.
*Source.* WM:1049; OQ-WM-34.

**CT-15. The chart while `transit_state != IDLE` (LEG, ARRIVING, BERTHING; the Pipeline's `TRANSIT_HOLD` included).**
*Rule.* The layer stays openable and is **view-only**. The pick is frozen (CT-8); nothing on the map is focusable or activatable; focus rests on the layer's Back hint (a text-labelled control). The state word `IN TRANSIT` (instrument face) is shown **iff `pod_at == IN_FLIGHT`**: a direct `web.view` read, no other condition. It sits at a **fixed position** inside the chamfered border at the top-left, anchored to the picture rect with the 5 % safe margin (PIPE:88), and is never placed near the origin node, the target node or along the route, because position would read as direction or progress that `web.view` structurally lacks. It appears and disappears in a cut with the data; no ellipsis animation, no dots, no blink. BERTHING shows **no** state word and no progress element (WM:1307: a berth is witnessed by the world). No pod marker is interpolated (WM:1048). **Nothing is drawn from `ChartPick` while non-IDLE**: the chart must never reconstruct the target from its own pick, because the pick resets on restore, is host-local and is wrong in co-op. The planned-sortie route in flight is drawn **only from `web.view.leg_target_id`, `leg_origin_id` and `leg_dir`** (WM-36; closes C12 and ASK-CT-3) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G2)*: on an OUT leg the dashed Tungsten line runs HOME (`HOME_ID`) to `leg_target_id`; on a BACK leg it runs from the node `leg_origin_id` (the departure derelict) to HOME. It is one segment between two node centres with no arrowhead, gradient, dash-phase animation or other direction mark, so it is the same line the pod-at-a-derelict case draws while IDLE (CT-10) and carries no progress. If the needed id reads NONE or names no node in `nodes[]` (for example an ABSORBED record that left the list, CT-12), no line is drawn and the `IN TRANSIT` word stands alone. BERTHING draws no line. The chart never calls `web.leg_timing()` (Audio-only, WM:300).
*Source.* WM:1048, :1268, :1307; WM-36; ASK-AD-7.

**CT-16. Layout contract across every reachable canvas.**
*Rule.* The layer is asserted at every reachable HUD canvas `picture / H`, with `H` an integer in `[H_default, 2 * H_default]`, `H_default = max(k, 3)` and `k` the integer **world scale** (PIPE D.4; **zoom never changes the HUD canvas**, PR-12: "H is independent of k and of zoom"). The required canvas rows are: 1080p / 1440p / 2160p `keep` at `H_default`, 640 x 360; 1080p at `2 * H_default`, 320 x 180; Deck 1280x800 `expand` at H 3, 426.67 x 266.67, and at H 6, 213.33 x 133.33; 720p at H 3, 426.67 x 240, and at H 6, **213.33 x 120, the smallest reachable canvas**. Layout is anchor-relative only (PIPE:88, 5 % safe margin) and **every layer-3 rectangle is positioned by anchors 0 or 1 plus integer HUD-px offsets**; a centred element is permitted only if its on-screen width is an even number of screen pixels. The panel is therefore `floor(canvas_w / 3)` HUD px wide, hung from a right anchor of 1, so every edge is an integer screen pixel at every `H` and "a third" holds to within one screen pixel (orchestrator ruling: the ui-programmer's integer rule over the ux-designer's fractional `canvas_w / 3`; D.1 in Formulas). Where the chart multiplier `m` (D.1b) is below 1 the chart switches to the compact layout (CT-44). Nothing scrolls at MVP's node and item count. At MVP only `H_default` is player-reachable (HUD scale is Settings', Alpha), but the contract and its acceptance criteria cover the full range through a forced-`H` test hook.
*Why.* The reflexion log (2026-10-26, "invariant — inner box unsatisfiable at zoom-in fields"): an invariant written against the default must be re-checked whenever a later decision adds reachable states.
*Source.* PIPE PR-12 (PIPE:224), D.4 (PIPE:369-386); `docs/consistency-failures.md`.

#### D. The panel, settings and the notice

**CT-17. The pause panel's content.**
*Rule* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: Save/Load's UI-2 was amended the same day to "no save fact on the pause panel"; the newest-record line, the playtime of the newest generation, is on the main-menu Continue screen only, read on every `MAIN_MENU` entry (re-read after a FAULTED return, a failed `new_game` or a Display & Comfort return, TX-34, TX-37; never refreshed while the menu stays open) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, because pausing is free and a line that changed between writes would show that a write is overdue)*. The panel shows the four items of CT-3 and **no save fact**: no playtime, no depth label, no countdown, no "time since", no gate state, no per-write mark (SL UI-2, AC-SL-133). Its content is identical across any number of autosave periods. It reserves one **status-line slot** (height `PANEL_RECORD_LINE_H_HUD`, id retained for continuity, D.1) that is **empty** except in `SAVE_FAILED`, where it holds the plain word `FAILED` (CT-21); because the slot's presence is constant, opening the panel never reflows. The panel has **no title and no state word**: it never says "Paused" (in OVERLAY the sim is running, and a word that is false in one mode is a lie on the truth layer). `SaveInfo.depth_label` does **not** appear on the panel (it is on the main-menu Continue line only; the Web Map & Transit row at WM:1309 saying "pause panel / save list" is satisfied by the save list, routed as C13). Panel plate: opaque Ground (HR-2), one chamfered Graphite-lit border, one rivet mark per corner (ART 7.4). The world beneath is frozen exactly as lit, not dimmed, blurred or desaturated (PIPE:265); a click on that world does nothing (CT-41). Panel item labels are in the reading face (ART 7.1 assigns settings and long menu copy to the reading face; panel item labels follow it as menu copy, orchestrator ruling closing ASK-UP-3; a clarification row to the art-bible owner); the `FAILED` word and the Continue line's numerals are the instrument face.
*Source.* SL UI-2 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, UI-4; ART 7.4; PIPE:265; WM:1309.

**CT-18. Display & Comfort page: exactly four controls.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
*Rule.* One page inside the panel's column (in-session) or the main menu's content column (boot-flow, from the Display & Comfort item of CT-23; the same S6, TX-36, TX-37) with four controls *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; master volume added 2026-10-27, user rulings)*: **display mode** (windowed / fullscreen / borderless), **reduced motion**, **reduce flashing** and **master volume**. Defaults *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: display mode and reduced motion are the pipeline hooks' defaults (PR-13; PIPE:301 "MVP exposes the hooks with defaults"); **reduce flashing is ON at first launch** (user ruling D79, OQ-CT-7, CT-19); master volume starts at its top step, which Adaptive Audio's master bus treats as unity (Adaptive Audio's call, CT-29). Display mode is a three-choice list whose current choice is marked by a shape plus a word (not colour alone); the two booleans are chamfered switches (ART 7.4) with an on/off **word**. **Master volume** *(Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20), D85)*: a single row holding a **stepped control** of `VOLUME_STEPS` notches (10 at authoring, `[PLACEHOLDER]`; a stepped control and not a continuous slider so that a gamepad has exact steps, the same reason display mode is a list): Left / Right (the MenuInput directional feed, repeating by D.9) or a pointer click on a notch changes it by one step, the current step is marked by a shape plus a numeral (not colour alone), and step 0 is a legal choice (muted) shown with its numeral and no warning. The page publishes the step through the store's change signal; Adaptive Audio maps step to master-bus gain (the law, the floor and the default are its own; this layer carries no dB figure and plays no sound of its own, CT-29). It is not a safety toggle, so it is debounced like display mode (CT-19). Changes apply immediately and persist (CT-19); there is **no Apply button and no revert countdown** (a countdown would be a timer surface; any revert-on-failure safety is Settings' / an ADR). After a display-mode change the page reads back and shows the **applied** mode (a failed fullscreen request shows windowed); a mode change resizes the window, so `k`, `H_default`, the canvas and the panel width recompute live, the page stays open and focus is retained **by item id**, the bracket does not nudge and no animation plays on the relayout. The page carries only labels, switches and their words; it has no explanatory paragraphs. **Never on any page:** zoom (PIPE:232), rebinding, resolution list, world scale, **HUD scale** (deferred to Settings & Input Rebinding, Alpha: `H_default` already meets the 21-px floor on every display by construction), frame cap, V-sync, audio other than the one master volume control, the HR-15 ambient step (Lighting's), any true-value overlay (never an accessibility option, ART 7.4). Master volume is the fourth control (OQ-CT-1: Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): added at MVP, D85) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The page's height is re-derived in D.1c.
*Why.* The first three controls (display mode, reduced motion, reduce flashing) are those an outside playtester needs and that PR-13(c)/(e) makes load-bearing; each is a boolean or enum hook the pipeline already defines. Master volume is the fourth because Display & Comfort is now reachable from the main menu (CT-23), so a first-time player can open the page before any world exists and would otherwise find no audio control at all *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. `Window` display-mode behaviour and failure reporting are NOT SOURCEABLE in the engine reference.
*Source.* PIPE PR-13, PR-14 (PIPE:228, 232), PIPE:301; ART 7.4.

**CT-19. Settings store.**
*Rule.* The four comfort values (display mode, reduced motion, reduce flashing, master volume) and the `notice_seen` flag *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* live in a host-local, per-install `SettingsStore` seam: **not a kernel block, not a command, not WORLD or PROFILE scope** (PROFILE is per-campaign, SL-9; settings survive New campaign). Nothing in the simulation reads it (HR-6: "no game state may read or alter" anchors, PCI:845). Consumers (Lighting, the Filter, Audio, the Camera) read `reduced_motion` and `reduce_flashing` through the store's read interface and a presentation-only change signal. A missing or unreadable store is treated as first launch with the first-launch defaults (reduce flashing ON, reduced motion at the pipeline default, master volume at its top step) and the notice (CT-20) is shown again (the safe direction) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)* **The safety toggles (reduced motion, reduce flashing) and `notice_seen` are written synchronously on the change**, before the next presented frame, so a crash or a mashed-through notice never leaves a player who switched protection on unprotected; only display mode and master volume are debounced (`SETTINGS_WRITE_DEBOUNCE_MS` `[PLACEHOLDER]`, Tuning Knobs) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* and it is flushed on page close, on Save & Quit and on exit. A write failure keeps the values in memory and retries at the next flush; **when the store cannot be written the layer shows one plain ship-log line once per process** (the first failed write, `ct.*` key, placeholder copy "Settings could not be saved."), never again in that process, so a read-only store does not nag on every launch. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: placement, lifetime and fit.)* **Surface:** the line is one `LABEL_1` reading-face line in the **reserved settings-line slot** of the surface that is showing when the failure is detected: on S9 (the notice) under the dismiss item; on S6 (Display & Comfort) between the last control and the Back hint (the slot is always present and empty otherwise, so it never reflows the page, CT-17's invariant; D.1c and D.1d include it in the fit math at the spatial rows). If the failure is detected while neither surface is showing (a debounced flush on page close, on Save & Quit or on exit), the line is armed and drawn in the slot of the next S6 or S9 that opens, or in the panel's status-line slot (CT-17) if the panel is showing and `FAILED` is not, and is never drawn at exit. **Lifetime:** it stays until that surface closes, is then consumed and never returns in the process. At the compact rows (`m < 1`, DEFERRED-REACHABLE) the S6 slot is not reserved and the line is logged only (OQ-CT-40). A capture is owed (AC-CT-136). The few-hundred-byte write is on the UI thread and no thread is introduced. **First-launch default of reduce flashing is ON** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): the creative-director's recommendation (the safe direction: a crash or a mashed-through notice otherwise leaves the player who needed protection unprotected) is adopted (OQ-CT-7, D79; the Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline amends its PR-13 hook default to match). The ruling covers reduce flashing only; reduced motion keeps the pipeline's hook default. A fresh store therefore reads `reduce_flashing = true`, and the effective re-open interval at first launch is `CHART_REOPEN_MIN_MS_REDUCED` (CT-3). The Chart Table registers zero blocks, hooks, commands and mutators (CT-27).
*Source.* KRN CR-16; PCI:845; PIPE:301. **-> ADR candidate (c):** the store mechanism (a `ConfigFile` at `user://settings.cfg` is the working proposal; NOT SOURCEABLE in the engine reference) and its Alpha replacement.

**CT-20. First-launch photosensitivity notice.**
*Rule.* Shown once, before the main menu, on the chart-table register, in the reading face (PIPE PR-13(e), PIPE:228). It offers the two toggles (reduce flashing, reduced motion) inline, with **reduce flashing shown already ON** (the first-launch default, CT-19; the player may turn it off here) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, and one dismiss action; no countdown, no auto-dismiss, no hold, no animated element (the notice is not itself a photosensitive stimulus), and **default focus on the dismiss action, not on a toggle** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the first input of the game is most likely a bare accept, and a state-flipping control under default focus would turn the shown-ON reduce-flashing protection OFF for exactly the player the D79 ruling protects. Both toggles stay reachable by navigation (NAV_UP from dismiss; the order is the two toggles, then dismiss), and a single `ACCEPT_DOWN` / `ACCEPT_UP` pair on first launch dismisses the notice, writes `notice_seen` and leaves `reduce_flashing = true` (AC-CT-131). **General rule:** no state-flipping control holds default focus on any screen reachable before the player's first deliberate input; this covers S9, the only such screen (S6, even when reached from the boot main menu, is entered through the player's own deliberate accept on the Display & Comfort item, and the CT-41(1) fresh-press latch keeps that press from activating its first control). The dismiss label must not be "Continue" (Save/Load's campaign verb, SL:993); it is a `ct.*` key. Dismissal writes `notice_seen` to the store. Copy is the UX spec's and the narrative director's; this GDD fixes only the behaviour. If the flag is absent the notice appears again; a second showing is identical (no state difference).
*Source.* PIPE PR-13(e); CON outside-playtester gate (CON:483).

#### E. Saving, campaigns and failures

**CT-21. Save & Quit (UI-2 and UI-3 behaviour, verbatim).**
*Rule.* One action calls Save/Load's `save_and_quit()`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the call's blocking behaviour is stated.)* **Call contract (this layer's requirement of Save/Load, ASK-CT-7):** the layer disables every item and **presents one frame** before it makes the call, so the disabled styling is on screen before any work; the call must return promptly with `REFUSED_DOWNED` (the same fact as `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED`) or `STARTED`, and report completion through a signal or the published `save_status` (`WRITTEN`, `FAILED`) rather than by blocking the frame loop for the write, because a blocked loop freezes the panel and invites an Alt-F4 mid-write. Save/Load's timeout `SAVE_QUIT_TIMEOUT_MS` is Save/Load's value, default 3 000 ms (range 2 000-15 000) since 2026-10-28; the time to a visible `FAILED` is that timeout, which is within this layer's 3 000 ms ceiling (nothing visible is allowed during the wait, SL-24, and a longer frozen-looking panel invites an Alt-F4 mid-write; ASK-CT-18 answered; Resolved 2026-10-27 — delegated ruling G2, creative-director lean, horror-tone reasons). This layer holds no timer of its own: it never times the write, it reacts only to Save/Load's published facts (`save_status`, `quit_held`, `quit_write_active`); `save_status` is sticky across earlier autosave failures, so `quit_held`, not a stale `FAILED`, is the trigger for a held quit. On `quit_write_active` rising while not already in `SAVE_QUIT_WAIT` the layer locks as TX-39. On `quit_held` rising the layer shows `SAVE_FAILED` as TX-23. When the player returns to play (Resume, `chart_table` close, Back to `CLOSED`) while `quit_held` is true or after a `UI3_PLATE` raised by `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED`, the layer calls Save/Load's `cancel_held_close()` once (idempotent); it does not call it when the exit ends in the Quit-without-saving hold *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6)*. AC-CT-137 and AC-CT-139 are the checks. **Save/Load's `save_and_quit` itself calls `request_shutdown` after `WRITTEN`, or after an unchanged-state-hash skip that writes nothing (SL-6); the layer calls nothing on that path and never waits on `WRITTEN` as an exit guard.** The item is enabled iff `PauseGate.can_save_quit` (CT-4); otherwise it is disabled (the Hull Grey disabled style of CT-VA-7). While the write runs (state `SAVE_QUIT_WAIT`, this GDD's own label and not a Save/Load name) all items are disabled, `chart_table` and `cancel` are ignored, and **nothing is added to the screen**: no spinner, no ring, no bar, no percentage, no toast, no text change (SL UI-2; SL H5; SL-24). If any bound seat is `DOWNED` or `DEAD` (the `DEAD` case is unreachable in the solo MVP, SL-6), nothing is written and the result `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED` opens **`UI3_PLATE`**, the UI-3 message plate: a string stating that the newest autosave will be used (Continue restores the newest verified generation), **Back** (default focus, non-destructive) and **Quit without saving** (the destructive item, under hold-to-confirm, CT-22). A held or failed write (`SAVE_QUIT_TIMEOUT_MS`, Save/Load's) lands in **`SAVE_FAILED`, which is the panel itself** with the plain word `FAILED` (a word, never colour, never Alarm Red: a save is not an instrument, HR-4) in the panel's status-line position (CT-17): the four items stay in CT-3 order, and Resume, Chart, Display & Comfort and `chart_table` all work (SL:785: "the pause panel stays"). Accepting Save & Quit there opens `UI3_PLATE` with **Retry** (default focus, non-destructive; to `SAVE_QUIT_WAIT`) and **Quit without saving** (hold). Completing the UI-3 hold calls `request_shutdown` once (CR-15) with no write; further input is ignored. `save_status` is a sticky read the layer never clears. `UI3_PLATE` is a full-bleed **message plate** (CT-28) in place of the panel *(orchestrator ruling: UI-3's string is a paragraph and the panel is too narrow for the 45-75 character reading measure at 4 of 6 canvases, ui-programmer finding X1 option (a); OQ-CT-12)*. No death language anywhere (SL:993 list).
*Source.* SL-6 (SL:195); SL UI-2, UI-3 (SL:998-999); SL H5, SL-24.

**CT-22. Hold-to-confirm gesture (closes SL OQ-32 by citation).**
*Rule.* Destructive actions (UI-3 quit without saving; UI-5 retire and delete *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*) fill a **linear bar with a needle-mark**, never a radial wipe (ART 3.4, 7.3), over the action's hold duration *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: `min(CONFIRM_HOLD_S, CT_HOLD_CAP_S)` for UI-3 quit-without-saving and the UI-5 Retire *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6)*, and `DELETE_HOLD_S` (= 2 x `CONFIRM_HOLD_S`, 1.6 s at authoring) for UI-5 delete; both are Player Controller-owned constants and the second is a **proposed name** (value and range in the Tuning Knobs "cited, not ours" rows; Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): D81, OQ-CT-30). Every hold-to-confirm also carries the **committed accessibility plan** (Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): D80, OQ-CT-8): a two-tap alternative (PCI:835) and an adjustable hold duration, delivered at Alpha by the Controller and Accessibility Settings; the MVP layer ships the hold only, but D.2 reads its threshold from the action's effective duration (never a literal) so neither option needs a rebuild of the gesture. The action fires on the first frame the held time reaches it, not on release. Default focus on a screen with a destructive action is its non-destructive item. Release, `cancel`, `chart_table`, the pointer leaving the item, or window focus loss snaps the bar to empty in the same frame and **nothing else happens**; a new gesture needs a new press edge. The fill is a monotonic fill on the layer's UI clock (CT-43), not a flash (HR-13), and it is function rather than decoration so reduced motion does not remove it. Formula D.2.
*Clarification.* PCI:656 says "hold timers (confirm bar, name reveal) are frozen with the pause". That sentence concerns the Adapter's **in-world** channel timers; the UI-3 bar must run while the kernel is `PAUSED`, because the Chart Table is the only thing awake then. Routed to the Player Controller as a wording clarification (C15).
*Source.* SL UI-3, UI-5; ART 7.3, 7.4, HR-14; registry `CONFIRM_HOLD_S`; PCI:835.

**CT-23. Main menu (UI-4).**
*Rule.* The boot menu has four items in fixed order: **Continue, New campaign, Display & Comfort, Quit** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: Display & Comfort is added so the comfort and photosensitivity toggles are reachable after the first launch without entering a live world, which is the reason the layer exists; it reuses S6, TX-36, TX-37, AC-CT-122)*. Continue is shown disabled (not hidden) when `campaign_info()` returns nothing; default focus is Continue if enabled, else New campaign. Continue shows exactly Save/Load UI-4's SIM facts as words, **without `RISKY`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: playtime (D.7, read on every `MAIN_MENU` entry (re-read after a FAULTED return, a failed `new_game` or a Display & Comfort return, TX-34, TX-37; never refreshed while the menu stays open) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*), crew count, kind (`AUTO` / `EVENT` / `QUIT`) and `depth_label` (`RING <n>`).
*`RISKY` is not on the Continue line — ruled* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): `RISKY` is dropped from the player-facing Continue line (OQ-CT-31, D82; the creative-director's lean is adopted). It is a fact about the **file** (the generation was written while a soft gate condition was closed, SL-6), not about the world, and it could read as a world-danger or tell-state leak onto the truth layer (a breach was open when the record was written) with no decision attached to it (a single Continue). It stays in `SaveInfo` and in the Debug Overlay; this layer neither reads nor draws it, and the Continue line is exactly playtime, crew count, kind and `RING <n>`. Save/Load's UI-4 is amended to match (hand-off note). **`RING <n>` is the ring of the newest record's pod / home position at the time that record was written; it is never a maximum, a historical "deepest reached" or any running aggregate over the campaign** (G5; a later record at a shallower ring shows the shallower ring; AC-CT-113). No thumbnail, no completion figure, no wall-clock "last played" beyond file metadata. The UI-7 line is shown under Continue on menu entry iff `campaign_info()` reports the newest generation failed verification (`newest_failed_verification`, accepted by Save/Load SL-21; ASK-CT-5 answered). One Continue serves the one logical autosave: the generation chain is invisible and never chosen (UI-4's own wording uses another noun; the stale-token lint allow-list carries it). Vocabulary is Save/Load's only (SL:993). `new_game(world_seed)` takes its seed from Save/Load's boot rule; the menu has no seed field (OQ-CT-6). The backdrop reuses the depth-ring plate and the ring-1 filament sheet with **no nodes** (a node would imply a save state or leak a Continue fact); fallback plain Ground. No title art, logo animation or tagline.
*Source.* SL UI-4 (SL:1000), SL-21 (SL:261); ART 2.11.

**CT-24. New campaign, UI-5, no beam picker.**
*Rule.* New campaign with no existing campaign calls `new_game(world_seed)` directly (via CT-42). With an existing campaign it opens the **UI-5 screen** itself: items **Back** (**default focus**), **Retire** (**under hold-to-confirm at `CONFIRM_HOLD_S`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: at MVP the player experiences it as irreversible, because Continue stops listing the campaign and there is **no un-retire screen**; the file stays on disk but nothing in the game brings it back, and no text calls it "reversible" or "archived"), and **Delete** as a separate explicit action on the same screen under the longer hold (CT-22). The UI-5 sentence (a `ct.*` key, copy the UX spec's and narrative director's) states plainly what each item does to the player's ability to continue: Back keeps the campaign and Continue still lists it; Retire (hold) takes it off Continue and the game cannot bring it back; Delete (longer hold) destroys the file *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; the word "Retire" suggests a recoverable archive, so the vocabulary is routed to Save/Load (OQ-CT-39) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the default moves from Retire to Back, because the old default let a double-tap on New campaign retire the campaign; the fresh-press-edge rule of CT-41(1) now applies to **every** boot transition, so the press that opened UI-5 never activates an item on it)*. Retire and delete call `retire_campaign(campaign_id)` / `delete_campaign(campaign_id)`; the retired state `RETIRED` is caused by this layer (SL T-9, SL:430). The retired file survives and Continue stops listing it (AC-CT-128). The Delete hold is **longer than the quit hold** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): Delete (UI-5) holds `DELETE_HOLD_S` = 2 x `CONFIRM_HOLD_S` = 1.6 s at authoring (OQ-CT-30, D81), while quit-without-saving (UI-3) keeps `CONFIRM_HOLD_S` (0.8 s). Delete is the one action that destroys the file with no recovery even by hand, so it asks for twice the hold of the quit that leaves the file on disk; D.2 works the arithmetic. The destructive items (Retire and Delete) look like every other list item (no red, no warning mark) and are distinguished by the bar only *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. No campaign list, Records page or un-retire screen at MVP (OQ-CT-4: a retired file stays on disk and is recoverable by file only until an Alpha screen; no text calls retire "reversible"). No beam-shape picker: New campaign calls nothing beyond `new_game`; seat defaults are Suit & Equipment's (SE-9: seat 1 `BEAM_WIDE`); a `NewCampaignOptions` seam is reserved for the VS-B picker (SE:189 calls it optional).
*Source.* SL UI-5 (SL:1001); SE-9 (SE:189).

**CT-25. Load-failure screens (boot-flow).**
*Rule.* UI-8 (`SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE`: a plain statement that no readable record exists, with New campaign and Quit, in that order) and UI-9 (incompatible build: message, New campaign and Quit, in that order, never overwrite or repair; the item order is the same on both screens, which removes the earlier flip between UI-8 and UI-9) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* are **boot-flow screens**: no pause is requested because the kernel is not `RUNNING` when the failure is reported (SL:1004 says "Chart Table issues the pause"; that wording is stale, C7). UI-7 (corrupt newest record) is one plain ship-log line under the Continue entry **iff** `campaign_info()` reports that the newest generation failed verification (`campaign_info().newest_failed_verification`, SL-21; ASK-CT-5 answered); otherwise the line is HUD & Suit Layer's on the first `RUNNING` frame (SL:1003 lists both owners; this GDD owns the main-menu instance only). New campaign from UI-9 calls `new_game(world_seed)` through CT-42 without opening UI-5; Save/Load creates a different directory and the `READ_ONLY` campaign is never retired, deleted or written (SL:752, SL T-9). Placeholder strings are Save/Load's (SL:1003-1005); final copy is this GDD's `ct.*` table with UX and narrative sign-off. No icon, no red, no dialog chrome beyond the standard Ground plate.
*Source.* SL UI-7..UI-9; SL:770.

**CT-26. FAULTED in release.**
*Rule.* The destination is the boot-flow **main menu** (UI-4) with Continue offered, focus on Continue: in `FAULTED` the kernel never writes or autosaves over an existing save (KRN:151), so the newest verified generation is intact. Whether a one-line notice accompanies it is OQ-CT-5 (routed to the kernel and Save/Load); this GDD invents no string. Dev builds halt with the Debug Overlay (not ours). PIPE:269 says release "returns to the chart table" and KRN:151 says "returns to the menu"; the wording is reconciled by this rule and routed (C14).
*Source.* KRN:151, :940; PIPE:269.

**CT-42. The load sequence: black before work.**
*Rule.* Accepting Continue, New campaign, Retire (then New) or Delete (then New): (1) all items are disabled and the layer's plate is removed in the same frame; (2) layer 1 black is shown (PIPE row 1: shown in `TRANSIT_HOLD` and `BOOTING/LOADING`); (3) at least one frame at full black is presented (the mechanism is the Pipeline's `TRANSIT_HOLD` guarantee, `t_hold_pad` of at least 120 ms, which the layer awaits before step (4); the layer does not count frames itself, and the test hook is the frame-capture hook of AC-CT-60; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*); (4) only then is `continue_campaign()` / `new_game(world_seed)` called; (5) the first `RUNNING` frame is a camera `CUT` (PIPE:264) and the layer leaves `LOAD_HOLD` for `CLOSED` (TX-33). Nothing is drawn between (2) and (5): no text, no tip, no bar, no spinner (SL UI-6). If the call returns UI-8 or UI-9 the layer re-enables itself on that boot screen (TX-11); if `new_game` (or any other call) fails it re-enables itself on the main menu with one plain ship-log line under New campaign (placeholder copy "Could not start a new campaign.", final copy the UX spec's and the narrative director's; TX-34, OQ-CT-17 closed). A load that fell back from a corrupt newest generation is not a failure: it proceeds to `RUNNING` and the UI-7 line is the HUD's on the first `RUNNING` frame (CT-25) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
*Why.* A restore called with a visible, focused menu would freeze that menu for the whole restore, and a long non-pumping call can mark the window "not responding" on Windows (NOT SOURCEABLE). Save/Load has answered ASK-CT-7: the kernel's restore and every `post_restore()` run as one blocking call inside `LOADING`, never chunked, and the one presented black frame is the Pipeline's `TRANSIT_HOLD` guarantee (`t_hold_pad`); the rule needs only that one black frame, never progress *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
*Source.* SL UI-6; PIPE:99, :264, :278.

#### F. Registrations, strings and access

**CT-27. Zero registrations; submits nothing.**
*Rule.* The chart table is an `INPUT_SURFACE` (KRN:111; PIPE:188; PCI:147) that **submits zero commands at MVP**; the class is kept for VS-B guest pause / save requests (KRN CR-15). It registers **no state block, no hook, no command, no mutator**, and introduces no `allowed_when_paused` type (so KRN CR-16's paused-allowed list is unchanged). It reads only `web.view`, `SaveInfo`, `save_status`, `restore_reason`, `campaign_info()` / `list_saves()` and its own host-local state; never `web.leg_timing()`. It calls only the kernel control plane (`request_pause`, `request_resume`, `request_shutdown`) and Save/Load's host-local API (`new_game`, `continue_campaign`, `save_and_quit`, `retire_campaign`, `delete_campaign`). KRN:715 offers "a chart-table command with a path array" as a `MAX_PAYLOAD` example: that example is stale (C3) because this layer submits no command.
*Source.* KRN CR-15; SL-21; WM:300; SL-17 (the zero-block precedent).

**CT-28. Strings, faces and fit.**
*Rule.* All user-visible strings are keys in a closed `ct.*` table owned here (`tr()`), ship-log register, no composed fragments (a value token such as a time is allowed inside one whole string), no exclamation marks, no emoji, Save/Load's vocabulary only. **Instrument face** (bitmap, integer `H`): a closed string set, declared in the `ct.*` table, of `IN TRANSIT`, numerals, keycap letters and short state words; **reading face** (vector, continuous scale): panel item labels, settings copy, the notice, messages and every sentence (ART 7.1). Type and contrast floors are ART 7.1's, by name (menu body, section heading, title, label and caption floors at 1080p / 1440p; text 4.5:1, glyphs 3:1). Each key carries a **fit class**: `LABEL_1` (one line at every canvas row), `LABEL_2` (up to two lines) or `PARAGRAPH` (message plates only, full-bleed Ground sized to the text with the ART 7.1 line-length measure). A lint measures each key with the real faces at each canvas row of CT-16 and fails a `LABEL_1` that overflows; a menu label is never truncated or ellipsised (a clipped "Save & Qu" on the truth layer would read as a lie) and never wraps to a third line.
*Source.* SL:127, :993, :1008; ART 7.1.

**CT-29. Audio: requests only.**
*Rule.* This layer owns and specifies no sound. It requests from Adaptive Audio a UI-bus focus-move cue, a confirm cue and the queued-re-open acknowledgement `UI_CHART_REOPEN_QUEUED` (CT-3) (AA:562: "menu and map sounds are UI-bus, ducked out of the world mix on open; pause suspends all sim-derived sounds"); nothing for open, save or load. `UI_CHART_REOPEN_QUEUED` is one-shot, on the `ui` bus, with no visual twin, no sanity-stage or Attention dependence, and identical at `CHART_REOPEN_MIN_MS` 334 and `CHART_REOPEN_MIN_MS_REDUCED` 1000; the Chart Table requests it on the press frame that puts a raise into the empty re-open queue, or replaces the queued raise with a different desired raise, once per queued raise; it is SILENT for an idempotent repeat of the same desired raise, for `cancel` of the queue, for the frame on which the queue applies the raise (the plate is the feedback), for a raise that applies at once (no lower within the interval) and for a `request_restore` that clears the queue (TX-27) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G2/G6: CT-3 queued acknowledgement, ASK-CT-20)*. Ducking on open and suspension of sim sounds on pause are Adaptive Audio's. Silent menus are acceptable at MVP. No sanity or Attention effect on menu audio. The master volume control (CT-18, Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): D85) only publishes a step through the `SettingsStore`: the bus, the step-to-gain law, the default, the muted state and any cue played at the new level are Adaptive Audio's, and the layer plays nothing itself *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
*Source.* AA:562, AA:1086; HR-6. *(provisional — Adaptive Audio owns the cues.)*

**CT-30. Accessibility at MVP (structural).**
*Rule.* Now: opaque Ground plates (HR-2), focus as a shape (HR-11), ART 7.1 type and contrast floors (HR-12), gamepad completeness of every flow including the chart snap and every destructive action (HR-14), no colour-only state (SL:1010), and an **accessible name on every interactive control** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier "a text label on every control" was false for chart nodes, which by CT-14 carry no label)*. **Every focusable chart node is a `Control` with an accessible name equal to its neutral ordinal** (the `ct.compact.slot_<n>` key form of CT-44, **nothing drawn**, so G6 and CT-14 hold in the spatial layout); a node is never built as a bare quad, so adding the AccessKit wiring later forces no rebuild. Every other control's accessible name is its visible `ct.*` label. Mouse and touch players get a signal for what is selectable through the **pointer cursor** change over selectable nodes only (CT-45; OQ-CT-9 closed). At Alpha (Accessibility Settings / Settings): AccessKit wiring (NOT SOURCEABLE in the engine reference; an ADR (e) spike item), hold-duration and two-tap alternatives for every hold-to-confirm (the destructive holds of UI-3 and UI-5; the Controller's two-tap option, PCI), HUD opacity floor, reading-face scale, HUD scale. **Accessibility tier: standard** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* (Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): OQ-CT-8, D80). `design/accessibility-requirements.md` does not exist yet and is to be authored later with `/ux-design accessibility` (it is not created here); until then the layer targets the structural compliance listed above plus the committed Alpha items: a two-tap alternative and an adjustable hold duration for every hold-to-confirm (CT-22), and reduce flashing ON by default (CT-19, a user ruling).
*Source.* ART HR-2, HR-11, HR-12, HR-14; SL:1010.

#### G. Negative requirements (each its own rule, each with an acceptance criterion in Acceptance Criteria)

**CT-31. No Attention surface.** *Rule.* No Attention indicator, meter, tier, hint or glyph on any chart, panel or menu surface, under any setting; depth rings are shown as place, not as attention; no Chart Table surface displays or sonifies the Attention level, tell progress or a Horror's proximity (AA:1515); no per-write autosave mark (its absence would be a hidden Attention meter, SL:965). *Source.* AT:1373, AC-AT-61, AC-AT-66; AA:1515 (Adaptive Audio UI Requirements, "depth rings are place, not attention"); ART 7.4.
**CT-32. No horror element (Pillar 3).** *Rule.* No horror, horror position, threat bar, proximity indicator, direction, off-screen arrow, countdown, "last seen" pin or target ring on any chart or menu surface, **under any accessibility setting**. *Source.* HE:377, :1353, :1358, AC-HE-119; Pillar 3 (Attention is shown in the world, never as a meter).
**CT-33. No loot value, no remaining-salvage count.** *Rule.* The Chart Table picks the target and shows only what the crew lit, and never reveals; this confirms Boarding's provisional contract (BS:334, Dependencies row 17b); no loot value, no remaining-salvage count (BS:334; BS:1204's wording "and the fuel the hop will cost" is overruled by CT-35, C1). *Source.* BS:334, AC-BS-47c (BS:1427), WM-28, ART 7.4.
**CT-34. No sanity indicator.** *Rule.* No sanity meter, bar, ring, number, icon or colour on any surface of this layer, including under accessibility settings; save and pause surfaces are never a hallucination class. *Source.* SN:1350, :1357, AC-SN-83, SL H6.
**CT-35. No fuel, time, ETA, danger or "recommended".** *Rule.* No fuel figure, travel time, ETA, danger rating or "recommended" marker anywhere on the chart; `web.view` structurally lacks the data and the chart never reads `web.leg_timing()`. Boarding's row BS:1204 ("the Chart Table shows what the crew lit and the fuel the hop will cost") conflicts and **Web Map & Transit governs** (WM:1049, WM-35, AB-5; routed to Boarding to reword). *Source.* WM:1049, :1299, :1305; WM-36; C1.
**CT-36. No save decoration.** *Rule.* No per-write autosave mark, thumbnail, spinner, percentage, death screen, "progress saved" toast or loading text or tip. Load is layer-1 black via `TRANSIT_HOLD` (UI-6), then a camera `CUT`; this layer adds nothing after a death-stub restore; the **HUD** owns one plain ship-log line on the first `RUNNING` frame, keyed on `restore_reason = DEATH_STUB`, through its `FIRST_FRAME_LINE` channel, with no death wording (placeholder copy "Restored from the newest record.") *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): SL OQ-46 option (b), OQ-CT-33, D84; a death-restart with no text can read as a crash or a rubber-band, which sits badly with the tone row "plain honesty about the cost". The line is the HUD's, so the layer still shows no death screen, counter or word *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *Source.* SL H5, SL:965, SL-24 (SL:267); SL UI-6; ART 2.10.
**CT-37. No inventory, weight or loadout screen.** *Rule.* No inventory, weight readout or loadout screen. The Sanity evidence list (SN:1350, SN:1357) names "the inventory" and "the death/restore screen"; neither exists (IDB R8; SE:1289; SL; ART), routed to Sanity (C8). *Source.* IDB R8 (IDB:1065, :1320); SE:1289, :1317.

#### H. Reserved slots and the rules added by the specialists

**CT-38. Reserved slots (no stub art, no layout space at MVP).**

| Slot | Owner and phase | Constraint carried |
|---|---|---|
| Crew beam-shape glyphs on the chart; left-edge text chat | Co-op Session / Chat (VS-B) | ART 7.4, 7.5; risk K1 |
| Knowledge Codex annotations | Codex (VS) | each annotation needs a stated effect on play (IDX:47) and is never a lore dump on the truth layer; PROFILE scope |
| Post-loss UI read at the chart table | Failure Contract & Re-wake (VS-A) | ART 7.4 "Loss" |
| Onboarding flow after the notice | Onboarding (VS) | `POD_NOT_READY` reserved in Web Map & Transit |
| Beam-shape picker | VS-B | `NewCampaignOptions` seam |
| Records page, un-retire | Alpha | OQ-CT-4 |
| Full PR-14 settings UI, rebinding, HUD scale | Settings & Input Rebinding (Alpha) | MVP hooks with defaults |

**CT-39. Narrow truth.** *Rule.* The layer displays only facts that no hallucination class can lie about. Every field it draws is listed with its source (`web.view`, `SaveInfo`, `save_status`, `restore_reason`, `campaign_info()`, the `SettingsStore`) and with the statement that no hallucination class touches it; a fact that any class can falsify (Graph state, entity presence or position, salvage contents or value, a crewmate's rendered form or location) never enters the layer. *Why.* A true copy of a falsifiable fact on a free, pausable menu would make that lie free to disprove, and P4 requires that checking cost time, risk or trust. Review test: *if a hallucination could make the world disagree with this field, the field does not belong here.* *Source.* CON:261-265; Player Fantasy "Under hallucination".

**CT-40. Constant drift, constant depth.** *Rule.* The filament drift step and the ring tones are independent of every simulation read and of Attention, depth, sanity and session time; the only inputs to the drift are the layer's UI clock and the reduced-motion toggle. *Why.* P3 names "filament movement" as an in-world Attention sign; a chart filament that modulated would be a meter. *Source.* AT:1373; ART 2.11.

**CT-41. Input hygiene around the irreversible press.**
*Rule* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: guards (1) to (3) and (5) are enforced by the Adapter's MenuInput feed, CT-45; the layer states the behaviour it needs and consumes the events)*. (1) **Fresh press edge.** When the layer is opened by a console press or by a released governor raise, every activation on it requires a press edge that begins after the open frame; the open press's release, its auto-repeat and any carried-over held state are ignored (the Adapter latches them, CT-45). (2) **The closing activation is consumed.** When `interact` on Resume, `chart_table` or `cancel` at depth 1 closes the layer, the Adapter emits nothing from that control, to the layer or to gameplay, until it is physically released and pressed again; gameplay sees no `interact` edge until a fresh press. The layer **cannot** enforce this alone, because the Adapter polls and resyncs on resume. (3) **Echo ignored.** The Esc / Start toggle's key echo is suppressed by the Adapter, so a held Esc does not flicker the layer. (4) **No click-outside.** A `POINTER` DOWN or UP on the frozen world outside the panel does nothing (the world is a held frame, and a click there would read as an in-world action). (5) **Focus loss.** The Adapter's `FOCUS_OUT` aborts any hold fill and clears navigation state; on `FOCUS_IN` the layer stays open with the previous focus and the kernel state is unchanged.
*Why.* The next `interact` edge at the pod console is `pod_hop`, the irreversible act (WM:1225). PCI resends intents on resume and keeps a hold-mode channel only if the button is physically down, so the latch belongs to the Adapter and the layer's requirement is stated here. Key-echo detection and focus-out notifications are NOT SOURCEABLE in the engine reference (ADR candidate (e)).
*Source.* WM:1225; PCI:656-658.

**CT-43. The layer's UI clock.**
*Rule.* Layer 3 keeps its own presentation-time accumulators driven by the render delta, independent of the kernel state, of `present_time` (frozen under pause, PIPE:265) and of `SceneTree.paused` (ADR candidate (a)): one per hold gesture (starts at press, resets on release, abort or focus loss) and one drift step timer and table index (`acc_us`, `i` of D.5) that persist across opens *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, so filaments do not rewind on re-open *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Every delta is clamped to `UI_DT_MAX_MS` `[PLACEHOLDER]` so a hitch cannot complete a hold or jump the drift. The clock is presentation time (KRN CR-11) and never enters the simulation; no wall clock enters any rule except these presentation timers.
*Source.* KRN CR-11; PIPE:265.

**CT-44. The compact layout and stable order.**
*Status: **DEFERRED-REACHABLE*** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: at `H_default` every supported window (minimum 1280 x 720, registry `MIN_WINDOW`) gives `m >= 1` (D.1b "Minimum window"), so a player cannot reach the compact list at MVP; it is built and tested through the forced-`H` hook and becomes player-reachable with the Alpha HUD scale or a window delivered below the minimum. This rule, S4, the neutral ordinals, ASK-CT-10 and AC-CT-30 / AC-CT-112 are tagged accordingly.
*Rule.* The layout is **compact iff the chart multiplier `m` (D.1b, the fit inequalities) is below 1**, i.e. the frame plus its margins no longer fits the canvas; nominally that is a canvas narrower than 320 HUD px (`COMPACT_LAYOUT_MIN_CANVAS_W`, `[PLACEHOLDER]`, **derived** as `CHART_FRAME` width / 0.9, with the height twin 180 = 162 / 0.9; informational since the fit form replaced it as the input to `m`) or shorter than its twin; it is chosen at open and on canvas change, never mid-animation. The list holds at most `N_max_compact` rows (3 at the smallest canvases, D.1b). Then the spatial map is replaced by a **vertical node list** in the ART 7.4 menu grammar: one column of chamfered-rectangle items, Ground interiors, 1-px Graphite-lit borders, the bracket and bezel thickening for focus, each item carrying its 24x24 node glyph at left (the unknown tick drawn inside the silhouette exactly as on the map) and a **neutral positional ordinal** at right (a slot mark, below). The ring plate's Ground, border and rivets remain; rings and filaments are not drawn. The planned-sortie relation is a Tungsten dashed rule under the picked row (no new asset, no fill). The `IN TRANSIT` word keeps its fixed top-left position. **The compact label is a neutral positional ordinal and carries no identity.** It is one whole `ct.*` key per authored layout slot (`ct.compact.slot_<n>`, `n` = the record's position in the layout file, HOME included; no composed fragment such as a word plus a number, CT-28), rendered as the plain ordinal for that slot. It must not be a name, a dialect, a template word or a status word, and it never varies with `status`, `kind`, `template_id`, depth ring or map distance: **an UNSEEN row and a SEEN row render the identical key form**, and a record's key does not change when it becomes SEEN, BOARDED, `berthed` or ORPHAN. The key is bound to the layout slot and is not renumbered when an ABSORBED record leaves the list (a gap in the ordinals is a fact the list already shows, and a stable slot mark lets the player remember which row is which). The ordinal order is lint-checked like positions (no correlation with any Web Map & Transit cost; AC-CT-112). This is the single permitted exception to G6 and CT-14 ("except the CT-44 neutral ordinal in the compact layout, which is not a name"); it is **not** precedent for any further label. WM:1044 (UNSEEN "no dialect, no tick, no label beyond what selection shows") is read as forbidding identity, which a slot mark does not carry (text-label rule of CT-30; **accepted by Web Map & Transit**: its UNSEEN drawing row names the neutral ordinal as the single exception, ASK-CT-10 answered). **Order is a ranking proxy and is therefore fixed:** the list is in stable authored order, HOME first, derelicts in layout-file order; it is never sorted by status, distance, fuel, danger, recency or seen/unseen; an ABSORBED record leaves the list (it is part of HOME now), an ORPHAN stays in its slot, non-selectable; the relative order of the remaining rows never changes. Rows are never truncated; if the list cannot fit, the canvas minimum was mis-set.
*Source.* ART 7.4; WM:1044, :1050; CT-12, CT-16.

**Snap-to-node (the chart's own navigation, D.3).** On the spatial map, a direction press from the focused node moves focus to the nearest focusable node whose bearing falls inside the pressed direction's sector (`SNAP_SECTOR_DEG` `[PLACEHOLDER]`), widening to a half-plane if the sector is empty, else staying; no wrap, no shake, no flash; every focusable node is reachable from every other; focus order is geometry only, never ranked, and default focus is HOME. Distances used for ordering are layout distances and are never displayed (WM:1049 and ASK-AD-7 untouched). Formula, variable table and worked example in D.3 (Formulas). Stick navigation is edge-triggered at the Controller's deadzone with auto-repeat (`UI_NAV_REPEAT_MS` / `UI_NAV_REPEAT_RATE_MS` `[PLACEHOLDER]`).

**CT-45. Menu input feed (Adapter-owned)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; new rule)*.
*Rule.* **The layer never reads a device.** The Input Adapter (Player Controller & Input PC-1(a)) owns every device read and publishes a presentation-side **MenuInput** event stream on **render frames** (the layer runs on its own UI clock while the kernel is `PAUSED`, CT-43; the Adapter's physics-callback sampling serves gameplay intents only). The stream is the layer's only input, because the Controller's locked lint allows `Input.`, `InputMap.`, `InputEvent` handling, `_input(`, `_unhandled_input(` and `is_action_*` only inside the Adapter's registered files (PCI Acceptance, source-scan criterion). Events: `NAV_DOWN{dir, source}` and `NAV_UP{dir}` (`dir` UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT; `source` STICK, DPAD, ARROW, WASD); `ACCEPT_DOWN` and `ACCEPT_UP`; `BACK`; `CLOSE`; `FOCUS_OUT` and `FOCUS_IN`; `DEVICE_CHANGED{device}`; `POINTER{kind MOVE|DOWN|UP|WHEEL, button, position in window pixels}` *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync)* (the unit is fixed by CT-45(f)).
- **The Adapter also owns**: (1) **echo suppression** (a held key's OS echo produces no second event; CT-41(3)); (2) **deadzone hysteresis** (`MOVE_DEADZONE` to take an edge, `MOVE_DEADZONE_EXIT` to release it); (3) **the cardinal-axis rule** (a stick yields one NAV direction at a time: the axis with the larger magnitude, a tie going to the vertical axis, and the taken direction changes only after the stick has re-passed `MOVE_DEADZONE_EXIT`, so a diagonal drift cannot alternate between two directions); (4) **latch-until-released** for any physical control whose press closed the layer (Resume's accept, `chart_table`, `cancel`): the Adapter emits nothing from that control, to the layer or to gameplay, until it has been physically released and pressed again. This is the consume-the-closing-activation guard of CT-41(2), enforced where the resume resync lives (PCI resends intents on `PAUSED -> RUNNING`); for a console press that opened the layer, the open press's release and repeats are latched the same way (CT-41(1)).
- **Navigation set, per device.** Stick (cardinal, edge at `MOVE_DEADZONE`), D-pad (four buttons), arrow keys, and WASD (the `move_*` physical controls). **Accept** = the physical controls of `interact` in this context: E, gamepad A, and Enter and Space as additional keyboard bindings of the context's accept. **Mouse buttons produce `POINTER` events only, never `ACCEPT`** (this GDD's requirement): LMB is not an accept in the `chart_table` context. The Controller's `chart_table` note excludes every mouse button (PC-2a, ASK-CT-15 answered, C23 resolved) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6)*; so a pointer click and a pad accept are two paths that cannot both fire for one physical press (EC-CT-15). **Back** = `cancel` (X / B). **Close** = `chart_table` (Esc / Start). The **D-pad is navigation** in this context: the d-pad zoom edges of PC-16 are emitted from `gameplay` and `inspect_card` only (the PC-2 context table: zoom ignored here), so no zoom edge reaches the layer and none must be discarded by it (EC-CT-18). A pad or Deck player moves the bracket on a vertical list by stick, D-pad or the keyboard equivalents, with no diagonal ambiguity.
- **Pointer.** The layer hit-tests `POINTER` events against its own rectangles; a click is an activation only when the UP lands on the item that received the DOWN (EC-CT-16; the full activation rules are in "Activation, reveal and focus targets" below) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; hover turns a label Tungsten and hides the bracket (CT-6). A selectable chart node shows the **pointer cursor** (a cursor-shape change only, no art, no label), which closes OQ-CT-9.
- **One focus model** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The layer's focus is the only focus, and the mechanism is decided here (option 1 of the re-review): **every layer-3 `Control` is created with `focus_mode = FOCUS_NONE` and `mouse_filter = MOUSE_FILTER_IGNORE`**, so the engine's focus owner is never a layer-3 `Control`, the built-in `ui_*` actions have no focus to move, and the engine's own hover and click routing never reaches the layer (all pointer input arrives as `POINTER` events, hit-tested by the layer). The layer draws and tracks its single focus itself as a retained control id (the bracket is drawn from that id). The **accessible-focus path** (CT-30) is separate from engine focus: the layer publishes the retained control id and its `ct.*` accessible name to the accessibility layer through the per-item accessible-name properties, with no engine focus involved; whether AccessKit exposes and announces a `FOCUS_NONE` control's name is **[UNV]** and is spike item (2). Layer-3 Controls take no raw `gui_input`, and the layer neither consumes nor relies on any `ui_*` action. The earlier "engine focus may be mirrored" allowance is **deleted**: a `Control` that holds engine focus is exactly what Godot moves on `ui_down`, so mirroring cannot coexist with AC-CT-118. The rejected alternative (option 2, the Adapter consuming `ui_*` in `_input`) would keep engine focus live and add a second consumer to the Adapter's registered files. Engine specifics (the 4.6 split of mouse/touch and keyboard/gamepad focus, `modules/ui.md`; `FOCUS_NONE` Controls and the accessible-name path) are **[UNV]** until the ADR (e) spike on 4.7.2.
- **Activation, reveal and focus targets** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (closes the input-model gaps of the replacement model). (a) **Holds.** A hold (Quit without saving, Retire, Delete) starts on `ACCEPT_DOWN` while the item is focused or on `POINTER{DOWN, LEFT}` on the item, and continues while that activation is physically down and, for a pointer hold, the pointer stays over the item; `ACCEPT_UP`, `POINTER{UP}` anywhere, pointer exit, `BACK`, `CLOSE` and `FOCUS_OUT` abort it (CT-22). A mouse-only or Deck-touch player therefore completes every hold flow by pressing and holding the item; a click released before the threshold does nothing. (b) **Activation edge.** Every non-hold activation fires on `ACCEPT_DOWN` (after the CT-41(1) fresh-press rule) or, for the pointer, on `POINTER{UP}` over the item that took the `DOWN` (EC-CT-16); never on `ACCEPT_UP`, and a pointer `DOWN` alone never activates. (c) **Bracket reveal.** When pointer motion has hidden the bracket (PC-13), the first `NAV_DOWN` or `ACCEPT_DOWN` after that motion only reveals the bracket on the retained focus: it moves nothing and activates nothing, so an unseen control is never fired; the next event acts. (d) **Disabled items are focusable but inert** (accept does nothing; the Hull Grey disabled style of CT-VA-7); in `SAVE_QUIT_WAIT` nothing is focusable and the bracket is hidden (CT-21). (e) **Focus targets after a transition:** TX-23 (to `SAVE_FAILED`): Resume; TX-25: Retry; TX-32 and TX-39 (to `SAVE_QUIT_WAIT`): none; TX-19 (back to `PANEL`): the item that led to the screen just left (Chart, Display & Comfort or Save & Quit); TX-37: the Display & Comfort item. (f) **Cursor shape and coordinates.** The layer may not call `Input.` (the lint), so it publishes a cursor-hint request (`POINTER` over a selectable node or an item, else default) that the Adapter applies (PC-2a, ASK-CT-15 answered). `POINTER` positions arrive in **window pixels** *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync)* (ADR-0008 § 5 pins the root `Window` with stretch disabled, `content_scale_mode = DISABLED`, so the root viewport's mouse position is already in window px and no inverse stretch conversion exists; the Adapter forwards that value unconverted as `cursor_px`, and the unit is no longer open, AC-CT-135); the layer subtracts the read-only `RenderLayout.picture_rect.position` (the HUD origin: `hud_rect` is pinned to the `k_base` picture rect, so the picture's top-left is HUD (0, 0)) to get picture-relative screen px, then converts to HUD px (`/ H`) for panel and plate rectangles and to art px (`/ k`) for the chart frame (CT-16), keeping the fractional part for hit-testing; a position outside the picture rectangle (the letterbox) hits nothing and is not an error. (g) **Read phase.** The Adapter delivers one render frame's MenuInput events in arrival order at the start of that frame, before the layer's per-frame update, so a press and the state it acts on are the same frame's.
- **Repeat.** Auto-repeat of a held NAV direction is the layer's, from `NAV_DOWN` and `NAV_UP`, on its UI clock (D.9).
*Why.* A layer that reads devices fails a locked lint; the guard on the irreversible hop (CT-41(2), EC-CT-20) can only be enforced where the Adapter polls and resyncs; and a single focus model removes the parallel `ui_*` path. The decision is owed **before ADR candidate (e)** is written.
*Source.* PC-1(a), PC-2, PC-13, PC-16, PCI source-scan criterion; `modules/ui.md`, `modules/input.md`. **ASK-CT-15 answered by PC-2a** (the Controller publishes the MenuInput stream, the echo, hysteresis, cardinal-axis and latch rules above, and the Enter and Space accept bindings) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6)*.

**ADR candidates (not decided here).** (a) pause / UI process mode: kernel `PAUSED` versus `SceneTree.paused` and layer-3 `process_mode`; (b) layer-3 ownership, the shared component library (theme, bracket, keycap, Ground plate), the two-scale mechanism for the bitmap and reading faces (decided: two sibling CanvasLayers, pixel face at layer 3 scale `H`, reading face at layer 4 scale (1,1); ADR-0008 s2.5) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync)*, and textured-quad drawing of routes and seams; (c) the host-local settings store and its Alpha replacement; (d) the pick-to-console submit seam; (e) menu input actions for the `chart_table` context, key-echo and focus-loss handling; (f) black-before-work load sequencing with Save/Load.
*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)* **None of the six ADRs exists.** ADRs (a), (b), (c) and (e) are written before the first story; (e) is written only after the CT-45 decision (the Adapter-owned MenuInput feed) is taken. ADR (b) carries two constraints from this review: in OVERLAY the HUD critical strip stays above layer 3 (the opaque panel may cover only the hand rail), and layer 2 is hidden under a full-bleed plate at MVP (D.10). **Engine items for 4.7.2 are not sourceable from `docs/engine-reference/godot/`** (no `scripting.md` or `core.md`; nothing on `ConfigFile`, `SceneTree.paused`, `Window` display-mode failure, key echo, focus-out, font-importer hinting or AccessKit property names). The first spike therefore targets what is new in 4.5 to 4.7 before any ADR is written: (1) the 4.6 split of mouse/touch and keyboard/gamepad focus, and that a `FOCUS_NONE` / `MOUSE_FILTER_IGNORE` layer-3 `Control` tree leaves the built-in `ui_*` handling nothing to move *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; (2) AccessKit accessible names on `Control`s, including on `FOCUS_NONE` ones (CT-30, CT-45 "One focus model") *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; (3) HDR output (4.7) and the SDR guarantee for layer 3, whether layer-3 pixels stay exactly the Ground and Hull Grey hexes with `hdr_2d` enabled; (4) the reading-face hinting default change in 4.7 (`ResourceImporterDynamicFont.hinting`, R-AD-11) at the floor sizes; (5) `gui_snap_controls_to_pixels` behaviour for the integer rectangles of CT-16 (AC-CT-111); (6) `CanvasLayer` draw counts under `Viewport.get_render_info` (AC-CT-45). The index effort for this system is re-estimated L or larger (OQ-CT-35); the three UX specs and `design/accessibility-requirements.md` are authored before `/create-stories`.

### States and Transitions

The layer is a small state machine on **layer 3**, plus a second axis, the `PauseGate` mode. It owns no simulation state: every state below is host-local presentation state (CT-27), none is in a snapshot, and after any `request_restore` the machine is rebuilt from CLOSED (TX-27). The kernel column is shown for SOLO; in OVERLAY every in-session cell reads `RUNNING`. **No guard in either table reads the kernel column** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (TX-38's former guard did and is rewritten); guards read `web.view`, `save_status`, `SaveInfo` / `campaign_info()`, `PauseGate` (`mode`, `can_save_quit`) or host-local state (CT-4).

#### States

| State | Entry | Exit | Transitions (see the table below) | Who may cause them |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BOOT_NOTICE` | boot with no `notice_seen` in the store, or an unreadable store (CT-19, CT-20) | dismiss | TX-1 | the player (dismiss, or the two toggles inline); the store (flag absent) |
| `MAIN_MENU` | boot with the notice seen; dismiss; FAULTED in release (CT-26); `cancel` from UI-5; `cancel` from Display & Comfort (TX-37); a `new_game` failure result (TX-34) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Continue; New campaign; Display & Comfort; Quit | TX-3..TX-6, TX-12, TX-30, TX-34 (entry), TX-36, TX-37 (entry) | the player; Save/Load (failure results, via `LOAD_HOLD`); the kernel (FAULTED return) |
| `UI5_NEW_OVER_EXISTING` | New campaign with an existing campaign (CT-24) | Retire (hold); Delete (longer hold); `cancel` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | TX-7, TX-8, TX-12 | the player |
| `UI8_NO_LOADABLE` | `continue_campaign()` returns `SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE` (CT-25; TX-11, from `LOAD_HOLD`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | New campaign; Quit | TX-5, TX-6 (when a campaign exists), TX-30 | the player; Save/Load (result) |
| `UI9_INCOMPATIBLE` | `continue_campaign()` returns the incompatible-build result (CT-25; TX-11, from `LOAD_HOLD`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | New campaign (TX-5, never via UI-5); Quit (TX-30) | TX-5, TX-30 | the player; Save/Load (result) |
| `LOAD_HOLD` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: exits added)* | the load sequence (CT-42) step 1 (TX-4, TX-5, TX-9) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | **exits: TX-33** (the first `RUNNING` frame, a camera `CUT`, to `CLOSED`); **TX-11** (a Continue failure result to `UI8_NO_LOADABLE` or `UI9_INCOMPATIBLE`); **TX-34** (a `new_game` failure or any other failure result, to `MAIN_MENU`, items re-enabled) | entries TX-4, TX-5, TX-9; exits TX-11, TX-33, TX-34 | the player (the accept that started it); Save/Load (result); not this layer's content: layer 1 black is the pipeline's, with no text |
| `CLOSED` | in-session `chart_table`/Resume/`cancel` at depth 1; TX-33 (the first `RUNNING` frame after a load); any restore; TX-35 (`cancel` on a console-opened chart) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | `chart_table`; a no-pick console press at a prompt that opens the chart (CT-10); both are governed raises (CT-3 re-open governor) | TX-13, TX-14, TX-18 | the player (`chart_table`); the Input Adapter's console seam (CT-10); a restore closes any other state into it (TX-27) |
| `PANEL` | `chart_table` from CLOSED (TX-13, governed); `cancel` from CHART (origin `PANEL`), DISPLAY_COMFORT (origin `PANEL`) or a message plate | Chart; Display & Comfort; Save & Quit; Resume; `chart_table`; `cancel` | TX-15..TX-17, TX-19..TX-21, TX-24 | the player |
| `CHART` | Chart from PANEL with `transit_state == IDLE` (entry origin `PANEL`, TX-16); a console press that opens the chart (entry origin `CONSOLE`, TX-18); both governed raises *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | `cancel` (to `PANEL` if the origin is `PANEL`, TX-19; to `CLOSED` if the origin is `CONSOLE`, TX-35); `chart_table`; `transit_state` leaving IDLE | TX-15, TX-16, TX-18, TX-19, TX-28, TX-35 | the player; `web.view` (a `transit_state` change) |
| `CHART_VIEW_ONLY` | Chart from PANEL with `transit_state != IDLE` (origin `PANEL`); TX-28 (keeps the origin of the `CHART` it came from) | `cancel` (by origin, as `CHART`); `chart_table`; `transit_state` returning to IDLE while open (TX-29, to CHART with focus on HOME) | TX-15, TX-19, TX-29, TX-35 | the player; `web.view` |
| `DISPLAY_COMFORT` | Display & Comfort from PANEL (origin `PANEL`, TX-20); Display & Comfort from `MAIN_MENU` (origin `MAIN_MENU`, TX-36) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | `cancel` (to `PANEL` by TX-19, or to `MAIN_MENU` by TX-37); `chart_table` (in-session only; a boot-flow origin ignores it, CT-5) | TX-15, TX-19, TX-36, TX-37 | the player; the store (applied-mode read-back; live relayout, focus kept by item id) |
| `SAVE_QUIT_WAIT` (this GDD's label) | Save & Quit accepted, `can_save_quit`, and the call not refused; Retry on `UI3_PLATE` (TX-32); `quit_write_active` (TX-39) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6)* | Save/Load's `save_and_quit` ends the process (SL-6), or `save_status` `FAILED` with `quit_held` after `SAVE_QUIT_TIMEOUT_MS` | TX-22, TX-23, TX-39 (entry) | Save/Load (`save_and_quit`, `save_status`); the player cannot cause or abort it (input ignored, CT-21) |
| `SAVE_FAILED` | `save_status == FAILED` or the held-write timeout (TX-23). It is the PANEL with the plain word `FAILED` in the panel's status-line position (CT-17); the four items stay in CT-3 order | Resume; Chart; Display & Comfort; `chart_table`; Save & Quit (to `UI3_PLATE`) | TX-15..TX-17, TX-20, TX-25 | the player; Save/Load (`save_status`) |
| `UI3_PLATE` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | Save & Quit refused with `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED` (TX-24); Save & Quit accepted from `SAVE_FAILED` (TX-25); Save/Load's window-close handler refused with `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED` (TX-38) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | `cancel` to PANEL (TX-19); `chart_table` to CLOSED (TX-15, SOLO resume); Retry (where offered) to `SAVE_QUIT_WAIT` (TX-32); press-and-hold Quit without saving to `HOLD_CONFIRM` (TX-31) | TX-15, TX-19, TX-25, TX-31, TX-32 | the player |
| `HOLD_CONFIRM` (sub-state) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | press-and-hold on the destructive item of `UI3_PLATE` (TX-31) or `UI5_NEW_OVER_EXISTING` (Retire, TX-7; Delete, TX-8) | fill reaches 1 (TX-9, TX-26); release / `cancel` / `chart_table` / focus loss returns to the screen that owns the item (TX-10) | TX-7..TX-10, TX-26, TX-31 | the player only (the layer's UI clock advances the fill, CT-43) |

The `PauseGate` axis: **SOLO (MVP)** calls `request_pause` on CLOSED to PANEL or CHART (TX-13, TX-18; both governed raises, applied together with the plate, CT-3) and `request_resume` on any in-session state to CLOSED (TX-15, TX-35; lowers are never delayed) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. **OVERLAY (VS-B, reserved)** makes no kernel call (TX-14); `can_save_quit` is Session / Save-Load's. Every other row is identical in both modes.

#### Transitions

| ID | From | To | Trigger | Guard (never reads kernel state) | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TX-1 | `BOOT_NOTICE` | `MAIN_MENU` | dismiss | none | write `notice_seen` to the store |
| TX-2 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | (boot) | `BOOT_NOTICE` | boot | store has no `notice_seen`, or is unreadable | show the notice; default focus on the dismiss item (CT-20) |
| TX-3 | (boot) / FAULTED in release | `MAIN_MENU` | boot, or the kernel returning to the menu | notice seen | CT-26; focus on Continue if enabled |
| TX-4 | `MAIN_MENU` | `LOAD_HOLD` | Continue | `campaign_info()` non-empty | CT-42 sequence, then `continue_campaign()` |
| TX-5 | `MAIN_MENU`, `UI8`, `UI9` | `LOAD_HOLD` | New campaign | from `MAIN_MENU` or `UI8`: no existing campaign (otherwise TX-6); from `UI9`: always (the `READ_ONLY` campaign is never offered for retire or delete; New campaign creates a different directory, SL:752, SL T-9) | CT-42 sequence, then `new_game(world_seed)` |
| TX-6 | `MAIN_MENU`, `UI8` | `UI5_NEW_OVER_EXISTING` | New campaign | an existing campaign | CT-24 |
