| TX-7 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | `UI5` | `HOLD_CONFIRM` | press-and-hold Retire | none | D.2 starts on the UI clock with `HOLD_S = CONFIRM_HOLD_S` (the completion edge is TX-9) |
| TX-8 | `UI5` | `HOLD_CONFIRM` | press-and-hold Delete | none | D.2 starts on the UI clock |
| TX-9 | `HOLD_CONFIRM` (UI-5) | `LOAD_HOLD` | fill reaches 1 | none | `retire_campaign(campaign_id)` (entered by TX-7) or `delete_campaign(campaign_id)` (entered by TX-8), then the TX-5 sequence *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| TX-10 | `HOLD_CONFIRM` | the screen that owns the destructive item (`UI3_PLATE` or `UI5`) | release, `cancel`, `chart_table` (CT-5), focus loss | fill < 1 | bar to empty in the same frame; nothing else |

**Reachability and closure checks (for the acceptance criteria).** Every state has an exit that does not depend on a hold: `HOLD_CONFIRM` exits by release; `UI3_PLATE` exits by `cancel`, `chart_table`, Retry or Back; `SAVE_QUIT_WAIT` exits when Save/Load's `save_and_quit` ends the process (SL-6) or by `FAILED` / the timeout of TX-23 (a Save/Load guarantee); the boot-flow screens each offer Quit. There is no state with no player input and no external exit except `SAVE_QUIT_WAIT` and `LOAD_HOLD`, both bounded by Save/Load results; `LOAD_HOLD` now has its exits in the table (TX-33 on the first `RUNNING` frame, TX-11 and TX-34 on a failure result) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. A restore from any state lands in CLOSED (TX-27), and the first `RUNNING` frame after `LOADING` is a camera `CUT` (PIPE:264). **The transition table is data.** It lives in `assets/data/ui/chart_table_transitions.json` (path provisional; one record per TX id with `from`, `to`, `trigger`, `guard_id`, `effects[]`), is the single source the layer's state machine is built from, and is the file AC-CT-93 walks; the expected-edge fixture `tests/fixtures/chart_table/expected_edges.json` (provisional) lists every `(from, trigger, to)` triple of this section, so a missing or extra row fails the build by diff, not by a walker's silence *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

**Order of presentation on boot.** TX-2 (notice) precedes TX-3 (menu); the menu precedes any session; the notice is never shown mid-session. FAULTED in release (TX-3) skips the notice when it has been seen.

### Interactions with Other Systems

Status words follow the Glob of `design/gdd/` at authoring: **designed** = a GDD file exists headed Designed; **in-flight** = a file exists in draft or a scratch directory exists with no contract; **undesigned-provisional** = no file. Line numbers are as read on 2026-10-26/27 and drift while sibling agents edit; rule and AC ids are given with them. **Downstream dependents among the systems designed in this run: none.** Settings & Input Rebinding (Alpha) depends on this GDD (IDX:210) and is undesigned. Each row quotes the neighbour's own row where one exists; "Consistent" means this GDD's behaviour matches the quote, and any mismatch is a numbered conflict in the Cross-References table or Open Questions.

| **Perception Filter** (designed) | none | none | truth classification | Filter owns the wrap | PF:223 (row 9): "HUD frame, menu, pause, settings, chart table, text chat, Debug Overlay | TRUTH (separate viewport; the pass sits below every `CanvasLayer`; the frame is fed nothing) | PIPE PR-3, PR-10; HR-6, HR-13; SN-14" | Yes (CT-2) |

The Graph, Item DB and Derelict Authoring name it only as "Web Map's" or not at all; Interaction names it in IA-40 and its Interactions rows (the seam is accepted; the POD and DOCK prompts are ASK-CT-13) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The systems index lists only Web Map & Transit and the Pipeline (IDX:95, :201): corrections routed to `/map-systems` (C5). The registry carries four "pending referenced_by" comments naming `design/gdd/chart-table.md` (line numbers not cited; the registry lines drift), a path that differs from this file; the merge stage rewrites them and drops the `HOP_FUEL_ML` pending reference or keeps it only for a negative sentence (C6).

## Formulas

**Scope and determinism statement.** The Chart Table owns no simulation state (CT-27), so none of the formulas below is hashed (kernel D.12 `state_hash`), snapshotted, replicated or fed back into the simulation, and none uses `rng_derive`. They are **host-local presentation arithmetic** on the layer's own UI clock (CT-43): floating-point is therefore permitted, and wherever a formula is integer-only (D.1, D.1b, D.2's accumulator, D.6, D.7, D.9) that is for **pixel exactness and test repeatability**, not for the kernel's determinism rule. The only simulation-derived inputs are read-only integers or enums from `web.view` and `SaveInfo`. Every formula below is evaluated at presentation time and may be called from a test double with no kernel running (CT-4, AC-CT-2). Dependency formulas are cited by registry or owner name, never redefined: the HUD scale `H` and `H_default = max(k, 3)` (Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline D.4, registry `hud_multiplier`), `CONFIRM_HOLD_S` (Player Controller, registry), `HUD_MIN_SCALE` (Pipeline, registry) and the art-bible numbers of section 7 (ART 7.3, 7.4). Notation: `a div b` is integer (floor) division of non-negative integers; `ceil_div(a, b) = (a + b - 1) div b` is integer ceiling division (registry `safe_margin`); `P_w`, `P_h` are the picture rectangle in screen px (Pipeline D.2/D.3); the HUD canvas is `P_w / H` by `P_h / H` HUD px and may be fractional (the Deck is 426.67 x 266.67 at `H = 3`). The six canvas rows are the rows of CT-16, numbered 1-6 in the order they are listed there.

### D.1 Panel fit

The panel fit formula is defined as:

`W_panel = P_w div (3 * H); safe_x = ceil_div((5 * P_w + 99) div 100, H); safe_y = ceil_div((5 * P_h + 99) div 100, H); content_w = W_panel - PANEL_INNER_PAD_PX - safe_x; V_need = 4 * PANEL_ITEM_H_HUD + 3 * PANEL_ITEM_GAP_HUD + PANEL_RECORD_LINE_H_HUD + n_wrap * PANEL_LINE_EXTRA_HUD; V_avail = (P_h div H) - 2 * safe_y; the panel fits iff V_need <= V_avail`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `P_w`, `P_h` | int, screen px | 1280-3840 / 720-2160 reachable | Pipeline D.2/D.3 (picture rect) | the visible picture, after `keep` or `expand` |
| `H` | int | `H_default .. 2 * H_default` | Pipeline PR-12, D.4 (cited; `H_default = max(k, 3)`, `k` the integer world scale, never the zoom step) | the HUD integer scale; at MVP only `H_default` is player-reachable (CT-18) and the rest is reached by the forced-`H` test hook (CT-16) |
| `W_panel` | int, HUD px | 71-213 over the six rows | this GDD (ART 7.4 "right third", integer rule of CT-16) | panel width, hung from a right anchor of 1; `W_panel * H` is an integer screen px by construction |
| `safe_x`, `safe_y` | int, HUD px | 11-32 / 6-18 | registry `safe_margin` (source HUD & Suit Layer; the 5 % is the art bible's, ab:754; Pipeline PR-2 cites it), cited, not owned here | the safe margin in whole HUD px, rounded **up** per registry `safe_margin` (never below 5 %); the frame fit of D.1b is re-asserted against the larger margin (AC-CT-26). *(Registry value, not an orchestrator ruling; the earlier floor form is retired.)* |
| `PANEL_INNER_PAD_PX` | int, HUD px | 2-8, default 4 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | this GDD, checkpoint ruling Q18 | inset between the panel's left edge and its content (chamfer border and rivet clearance); `PANEL_PAD_HUD` is retired |
| `content_w` | int, HUD px | 56-177 over the six rows | calculated | the width a panel label may use; lint-checked by D.8 |
| `PANEL_ITEM_H_HUD`, `PANEL_ITEM_GAP_HUD`, `PANEL_RECORD_LINE_H_HUD`, `PANEL_LINE_EXTRA_HUD` | int, HUD px | 12-20 / 2-6 / 8-12 / 6-10; defaults 16 / 3 / 10 / 8 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | this GDD | item height, gap, status-line slot height (the id keeps its old name; the slot is empty except for `FAILED`, CT-17), and the extra height of one wrapped line *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `n_wrap` | int | 0-4 | the `ct.*` fit classes (CT-28) | total extra wrapped lines across the four items (`LABEL_2` items; a third line is a lint failure) |
| `V_need`, `V_avail` | int, HUD px | 83-115 / 108-324 | calculated | stacked height needed and the vertical space inside the safe margin |

**Output Range:** `W_panel` 71 to 213 HUD px, i.e. 426 to 639 screen px; `content_w` 56 to 177 HUD px (336 to 531 screen px); the world strip left of the panel is `P_w - W_panel * H`, never below 854 screen px at 1280 wide. The panel fits at all six rows with `n_wrap = 0`; at the smallest canvas (row 6) it tolerates at most `floor((108 - 83) / 8) = 3` wrapped lines in total.

| Row | Picture, `k`, `H` | Canvas (HUD px) | `W_panel` (screen px) | `safe_x` / `safe_y` | `content_w` (screen px) | `V_avail` | Chart multiplier `m` (D.1b) | Chart layout |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 1920x1080 keep, 3, 3 (also 2560x1440 at `H` 4 and 3840x2160 at `H` 6: same canvas) | 640 x 360 | 213 (639) | 32 / 18 | 177 (531) | 324 | 2 | spatial |
| 2 | 1920x1080 keep, 3, 6 | 320 x 180 | 106 (636) | 16 / 9 | 86 (516) | 162 | 1 | spatial (exact fit) |
| 3 | 1280x800 expand (Deck), 2, 3 | 426.67 x 266.67 | 142 (426) | 22 / 14 | 116 (348) | 238 | 1 | spatial |
| 4 | 1280x800 expand (Deck), 2, 6 | 213.33 x 133.33 | 71 (426) | 11 / 7 | 56 (336) | 119 | 0 | **compact** |
| 5 | 1280x720 keep, 2, 3 | 426.67 x 240 | 142 (426) | 22 / 12 | 116 (348) | 216 | 1 | spatial |
| 6 | 1280x720 keep, 2, 6 | **213.33 x 120 (smallest)** | 71 (426) | 11 / 6 | 56 (336) | 108 | 0 | **compact** |

**Example (row 3, then row 6).** Row 3: `W_panel = 1280 div 9 = 142`, so the panel is `142 * 3 = 426` screen px and its left edge is at `1280 - 426 = 854`, an integer; a literal one-third anchor would put the edge at 853.33 and make the 1-px bezel and seam one screen pixel thicker on one side under `gui_snap_controls_to_pixels` (the engine's rounding rule is NOT SOURCEABLE, so the layout does not depend on it). `safe_x = ceil_div((5 * 1280 + 99) div 100, 3) = ceil_div(64, 3) = 22`; `content_w = 142 - 4 - 22 = 116` HUD px = 348 screen px. Row 6: `W_panel = 1280 div 18 = 71`; `safe_y = ceil_div((5 * 720 + 99) div 100, 6) = ceil_div(36, 6) = 6`; `V_avail = 120 - 12 = 108`; `V_need = 4 * 16 + 3 * 3 + 10 + 0 = 83`, slack 25, so the panel fits; with all four items wrapped once (`n_wrap = 4`) `V_need = 83 + 32 = 115 > 108` and the lint fails the string set. Row 1: `W_panel = 1920 div 9 = 213` (639 screen px, one screen pixel short of a true third, invisible).

### D.1b Chart frame, chart multiplier and the compact breakpoint

The chart frame formula is defined as:

`W_c = P_w div H; H_c = P_h div H; safe_x, safe_y from D.1 (margin rounded up); m = clamp(min(max(0, W_c - 2 * safe_x) div CHART_FRAME_W, max(0, H_c - 2 * safe_y) div CHART_FRAME_H), 0, CHART_M_MAX); compact iff m < 1; x0 = (W_c - CHART_FRAME_W * m) div 2; y0 = (H_c - CHART_FRAME_H * m) div 2; node_hud(i) = (x0 + m * pos_x(i), y0 + m * pos_y(i)); S_MIN = 24 + 2 * BRACKET_PAD_HUD; layout lint: for every pair i != j of focusable records, |pos(i) - pos(j)| >= S_MIN` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `m` is defined directly from the fit inequalities; the earlier `P_w div (MIN_W * H)` form floored the minimum canvas while the margin rounds up, so the frame overflowed by one HUD px at, for example, 1920x1083 and 1921x1080)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CHART_FRAME_W`, `CHART_FRAME_H` | int, art px (= HUD px at multiplier 1) | 288 / 162 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, derived: the smallest spatial canvas (320 x 180, row 2) reduced by the 5 % margin on each axis | this GDD (checkpoint ruling Q7) | the fixed frame in which node positions are authored (CT-12) |
| `MIN_W`, `MIN_H` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: informational)* | int, HUD px | 320 / 180, **derived** | this GDD (checkpoint ruling Q8) | the **nominal** breakpoint at exactly a 5 % margin, `(10 * CHART_FRAME_W) div 9` and its height twin; `MIN_W` is `COMPACT_LAYOUT_MIN_CANVAS_W`. **No longer an input to `m`** (that is the fit inequalities above); kept for the knob table, the registry note and the reader's orientation, and still moves with the frame |
| `CHART_M_MAX` | int | 2, fixed | this GDD | the cap on `m` (`H >= k` bounds it at 2 on every reachable canvas; a forced-`H` or an odd window cannot buy a third multiplier) |
| `W_c`, `H_c` | int, HUD px | floor of the canvas | calculated | the integer HUD canvas |
| `m` | int | 0-2 reachable (H >= k bounds it at 2) | calculated | the chart multiplier, **the largest integer for which the frame plus both margins fits the integer canvas**, applied to positions only; glyphs stay 24x24 at `H` (CT-12) |
| `x0`, `y0` | int, HUD px | `>= safe_x`, `>= safe_y` when `m >= 1` | calculated | frame origin, whole HUD px; rings and filaments extend full-bleed outside the frame |
| `pos_x(i)`, `pos_y(i)` | int, art px | `0 .. CHART_FRAME_W - 1`, `0 .. CHART_FRAME_H - 1` | `chart_layout.json` (CT-12), authored per `derelict_id` | node centre in frame-local px; never derived from `world_slot`, legs, ticks or distance |
| `BRACKET_PAD_HUD` | int, HUD px | 2-6, default 4 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | this GDD | clearance between a 24x24 glyph and its focus bracket |
| `S_MIN` | int, HUD px | 28-36, default 32, derived | calculated | the minimum centre-to-centre distance so brackets never overlap neighbours |

**Output Range:** `m` is 0, 1 or 2 over every reachable canvas (`H >= k` bounds it at 2); the layout is spatial iff `m >= 1`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)* With `m` defined from the fit inequalities the frame fit is **true by construction on every integer picture**: `CHART_FRAME_W * m + 2 * safe_x <= W_c` and `CHART_FRAME_H * m + 2 * safe_y <= H_c` hold whenever `m >= 1`, and `m` is the largest such integer (capped at `CHART_M_MAX`), so the frame never leaves the safe area (AC-CT-26, and the property test AC-CT-126). The earlier assertion "per reachable canvas row" failed off the six rows because the old `MIN_W` floored while the margin rounds up. **Minimum window.** The window is refused below 1280 x 720 (`Window.min_size`, registry `MIN_WINDOW`, Pipeline PR-1), and at `H_default` (the only MVP-reachable `H`) every supported window gives `m >= 1`: 1280 x 720 gives a 426 x 240 canvas, `safe_x` 22, `safe_y` 12, so `m = 1`. **The compact layout is therefore unreachable at MVP**; it appears only through the forced-`H` hook, the Alpha HUD scale, or a delivered window below the minimum (Pipeline: `k` clamped to 2, picture cropped), so CT-44, S4, the neutral ordinals and AC-CT-30 / AC-CT-112 carry the tag **DEFERRED-REACHABLE** (built and tested through the hook, not reachable by a player at MVP). **Compact capacity.** `N_max_compact = floor((V_avail + PANEL_ITEM_GAP_HUD) / (COMPACT_ITEM_H_HUD + PANEL_ITEM_GAP_HUD))` with `COMPACT_ITEM_H_HUD` 28 (`[PLACEHOLDER]`: the 24-px glyph plus 2 px above and below) is 3 at rows 4 and 6 (`V_avail` 119 and 108), which exactly holds the MVP roster (HOME plus two derelicts) and nothing more; a larger roster fails the layout lint (EC-CT-55) until OQ-CT-14 is answered. **Why the integer form:** `0.9 * 320` is not exactly representable in binary floating point and the 1080p `H = 6` canvas (exactly 320 x 180) sits on the boundary.
**Node inset and ring membership lint** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `NODE_INSET_ART = 12 + BRACKET_PAD_HUD` (16 by default). Every focusable record satisfies `NODE_INSET_ART <= pos_x <= CHART_FRAME_W - NODE_INSET_ART` and `NODE_INSET_ART <= pos_y <= CHART_FRAME_H - NODE_INSET_ART`, so the bracket and its pad never leave the frame at any `m >= 1` (the pad is in HUD px, positions scale with `m`, so the margin only grows with `m`); and the padded glyph rectangle plus its Ground clearing lies wholly in the ring-1 band (outside the ring-2 outer radius of `chart_layout.json`), so "all MVP nodes sit in ring 1" is a lint, not a hope.

**Example** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: recomputed with the fit-inequality `m`; the six rows are unchanged)*. Row 2 (1920x1080, `H = 6`): `W_c = 320`, `H_c = 180`, `safe_x = 16`, `safe_y = 9`; `m = min((320 - 32) div 288, (180 - 18) div 162) = min(1, 1) = 1`, spatial (the boundary case), `x0 = (320 - 288) div 2 = 16 = safe_x`, `y0 = (180 - 162) div 2 = 9 = safe_y`. Row 3 (Deck, `H = 3`): `W_c = 426`, `H_c = 266`, `safe_x = 22`, `safe_y = 14`; `m = min((426 - 44) div 288, (266 - 28) div 162) = min(1, 1) = 1`, `x0 = (426 - 288) div 2 = 69`, `y0 = (266 - 162) div 2 = 52`; an illustrative node at frame position (210, 60) is drawn at HUD px (279, 112). Row 1: `m = 2`, `x0 = 32`, `y0 = 18`, the same node at (452, 138). Row 6 (720p, `H = 6`): `W_c = 213`, `H_c = 120`, `safe_y = 6`; `m = min((213 - 22) div 288, (120 - 12) div 162) = 0`, **compact** (deferred-reachable). **Off-row counterexamples that broke the old form:** 1920 x 1083 `expand`, `k = 3`, `H = 3`: `W_c = 640`, `H_c = 361`, `safe_y = ceil_div((5 * 1083 + 99) div 100, 3) = ceil_div(55, 3) = 19`; the old form gave `m = min(1920 div 960, 1083 div 540) = 2` and `162 * 2 + 2 * 19 = 362 > 361`, an overflow of one HUD px; the new form gives `m = min((640 - 2 * 32) div 288, (361 - 38) div 162) = min(2, 1) = 1`. And `P_w = 1921` at `P_h = 1080`, `H = 3`: `W_c = 640`, `safe_x = ceil_div((5 * 1921 + 99) div 100, 3) = ceil_div(97, 3) = 33`, `288 * 2 + 66 = 642 > 640` under the old `m = 2`; the new form gives `m = 1`. Separation lint with illustrative positions HOME (144, 100), A (210, 60), B (80, 40) (not authored content): HOME-A 77.2, HOME-B 87.7, A-B 131.5, all >= 32. Content-lint corollaries: every record has a position, a `home_attach` offset and a stable ordinal, no position is read from `world_slot`, and no authored distance correlates with any Web Map & Transit cost (CT-12).

**D.1c Display & Comfort page fit** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; the S6 page had no fit math)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. `ITEM_H_S6 = min(PANEL_ITEM_H_HUD, max(S6_ITEM_H_MIN_HUD, (V_avail - (N_ROWS_S6 - 1) * PANEL_ITEM_GAP_HUD - BACK_HINT_H_HUD - n_wrap_S6 * PANEL_LINE_EXTRA_HUD) div N_ROWS_S6))`; `V_need_S6 = N_ROWS_S6 * ITEM_H_S6 + (N_ROWS_S6 - 1) * PANEL_ITEM_GAP_HUD + BACK_HINT_H_HUD + n_wrap_S6 * PANEL_LINE_EXTRA_HUD`; the page fits iff `V_need_S6 <= V_avail` of D.1. `N_ROWS_S6` = **6** (the three-choice display-mode list is three rows, plus the two switches, plus the master-volume row of D85), `BACK_HINT_H_HUD` = 10 HUD px (`[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 8-12, the Back hint line), `S6_ITEM_H_MIN_HUD` = 12 HUD px (the lower bound of `PANEL_ITEM_H_HUD`'s range, fixed), `n_wrap_S6` the wrapped lines of the page's `ct.*` keys. **Derivation of the shrink.** The constraint is that the page fits at every canvas row with `n_wrap_S6 = 0`. Before the master-volume row the page needed `5 * 16 + 4 * 3 + 10 = 102` HUD px and fitted row 6 (`V_avail` 108) with 6 to spare. One more row at the default item height costs `16 + 3 = 19`, so six unshrunk rows need `6 * 16 + 5 * 3 + 10 = 121`: that fits rows 1, 2, 3 and 5 (`V_avail` 324, 162, 238, 216) but not rows 4 and 6 (119 and 108), which are the compact, DEFERRED-REACHABLE rows (D.1b; unreachable by a player at MVP). The item height therefore shrinks on those rows, and only there, to `(V_avail - 5 * 3 - 10) div 6`. **Worked example.** Row 6 (`V_avail` 108): `(108 - 15 - 10) div 6 = 83 div 6 = 13`, so `ITEM_H_S6 = min(16, max(12, 13)) = 13` and `V_need_S6 = 6 * 13 + 15 + 10 = 103 <= 108`, slack 5. Row 4 (`V_avail` 119): `(119 - 25) div 6 = 15`, `V_need_S6 = 90 + 15 + 10 = 115 <= 119`, slack 4. Rows 1, 2, 3, 5: the quotient is above 16, so `ITEM_H_S6 = 16` and `V_need_S6 = 121`. Because the slack at rows 4 and 6 (4 and 5) is below `PANEL_LINE_EXTRA_HUD` (8), **no key on the page may wrap at those rows** (`n_wrap_S6 = 0`; the fit-class lint treats the page's keys as `LABEL_1` there); at rows 1, 2, 3 and 5 the slack (203, 41, 117, 95) tolerates wrapped lines. The 13 and 15 HUD-px items are at H = 6, i.e. 78 and 90 screen px, above any target floor the page needs; the art-director confirms the shrunken height against the ART 7.1 label floor (hand-off note). The page lives in the panel's column in-session and in the main menu's content column at boot, where the width is not the 71-px panel. Coupled constraint C-3 (Tuning Knobs) refuses a violating config at load as `CONTENT_INVARIANT` *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix)*. **Master-volume width term** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the volume row holds `VOLUME_STEPS` notches at a fixed pitch plus one numeral, `W_vol = VOLUME_STEPS * VOLUME_NOTCH_PITCH_HUD + VOLUME_NUMERAL_W_HUD <= content_w_S6`, with `VOLUME_NOTCH_PITCH_HUD` = 3 HUD px (fixed, a 1-px notch and a 2-px gap) and `VOLUME_NUMERAL_W_HUD` = 10 HUD px (two digits of the 5-px-cap instrument face at 4 px plus a 2-px gap; `[UNV]` until the bitmap faces exist). `content_w_S6` is the page's column width less `PANEL_INNER_PAD_PX` (56 HUD px at the 71-px panel of rows 4 and 6, 86 at row 2, 116 at rows 3 and 5). At the default 10 steps `W_vol` = 40 (fits every row); at 20 steps it would be 70, which does **not** fit 56 HUD px, so the range of `VOLUME_STEPS` is narrowed to **5-15** (`W_vol` = 55 <= 56 at the maximum; constraint C-8). **Settings-write-failure line slot** (CT-19; one `LABEL_1` line, `SETTINGS_LINE_H_HUD` = 10 HUD px `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 8-12): at the spatial rows (1, 2, 3, 5: `m >= 1`) S6 reserves this slot between the last control and the Back hint, always present and empty unless the one-per-process line is showing, so the line never reflows the page (CT-17's invariant); the page then needs `V_need_S6 + PANEL_ITEM_GAP_HUD + SETTINGS_LINE_H_HUD` = 121 + 13 = 134 HUD px at the defaults, which is at most `V_avail` at every spatial row (162, 216, 238, 324). At the compact rows (4 and 6, DEFERRED-REACHABLE) the slack is 4 and 5 HUD px, the slot is **not** reserved and the line is not drawn there (the failure is logged; OQ-CT-40).

### D.1d Boot-flow screen fit *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; new)*

The boot-flow screens (S9 notice, S10 main menu with its S6 and UI-7 content, S11 UI-5, S13 UI-8, S14 UI-9, S15) are full-bleed Ground plates with no world behind them, drawn inside the same registry `safe_margin`. The first screen any new player sees is the notice, at the smallest reachable canvas (1280 x 720, row 5 at `H = 3`, or the Deck row 3), so it needs its own fit proof. The fit is stated in the units of D.1 and D.8 and is a lint on the string set, like D.8.

`W_boot = (P_w div H) - 2 * safe_x; V_boot = (P_h div H) - 2 * safe_y; W_rail = W_boot div 3; W_col = W_boot - W_rail - BOOT_COL_GAP_HUD; content_w_boot = W_col - PANEL_INNER_PAD_PX; the main menu fits iff 4 * PANEL_ITEM_H_HUD + 3 * PANEL_ITEM_GAP_HUD <= V_boot (the rail) and its content column fits V_need_S6' (D.1c) or the Continue facts plus the UI-7 line; the notice fits iff n_lines(paragraph, W_boot * H) * LINE_H_PX + 3 * PANEL_ITEM_H_HUD * H + 3 * PANEL_ITEM_GAP_HUD * H + SETTINGS_LINE_H_HUD * H <= V_boot * H, with n_lines from the greedy wrap of D.8`

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `W_boot`, `V_boot` | int, HUD px | 191-576 / 108-324 over the reachable rows | calculated from D.1 | the area inside the safe margin; `V_boot` equals D.1's `V_avail`, so the S6 page at boot has D.1c's vertical fit unchanged |
| `W_rail`, `W_col` | int, HUD px | calculated | this GDD | the left navigation rail (a third, the same register as the right-third panel) and the content column |
| `BOOT_COL_GAP_HUD` | int, HUD px | 2-8, default 4 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | this GDD | the gutter between rail and content column |
| `LINE_H_PX` | int, screen px | `ceil(LINE_HEIGHT_EM * em_px)` with `LINE_HEIGHT_EM` 1.35 (`[UNV]`, estimate until the reading face is imported) | ART 7.1 (floors cited; sub-1080p floors OQ-CT-13) | the reading-face line height; reading text is sized in screen px, not HUD px (CT-UI-8) |
| `n_lines(paragraph, W)` | int | 1-12 | D.8 greedy wrap | the notice paragraph's wrapped lines at width `W` |

**Example (row 5, 1280 x 720 keep, `H = 3`; estimates flagged `[UNV]`).** `safe_x = 22`, `safe_y = 12`: `W_boot = 426 - 44 = 382` HUD px, `V_boot = 240 - 24 = 216`, `W_rail = 127`, `W_col = 382 - 127 - 4 = 251`. The rail needs `4 * 16 + 3 * 3 = 73 <= 216`. The notice: a 220-character paragraph at `em_px` 20 (`ADV_EM` 0.5) wraps at `W_boot * H = 1146` screen px into `ceil(220 * 10 / 1146) = 2` lines (`LINE_H_PX` 27), so it needs `2 * 27 + 3 * 48 + 3 * 9 + 30 = 255 <= 216 * 3 = 648`, slack 393 screen px, so the notice has room for a paragraph of up to `floor((648 - 201) / 27) = 16` lines. At the Deck row 3 (`H = 3`, canvas 426.67 x 266.67): `V_boot = 267 - 28 = 238`, more room still. At the forced-`H` rows (2, 4, 6) `H` doubles, items are 96 screen px and `V_boot * H` is 648 (row 6): `3 * 96 + 3 * 18 + 60 = 402` leaves `floor(246 / 27) = 9` paragraph lines at row 6. The lint rejects a notice paragraph above the line limit of the smallest row it is run against (AC-CT-134). The notice's **toggles and dismiss item reach every row** because their heights are D.1's item heights.

The hold-to-confirm fill formula is defined as:

`t_us(n+1) = t_us(n) + min(dt_us, UI_DT_MAX_MS * 1000) while the activation is physically down and the pointer or focus stays on the item; fill = clamp(t_us / (HOLD_S * 1000000), 0, 1); needle_px = min(floor(fill * L_BAR_HUD), L_BAR_HUD - 1); the action fires on the first frame with t_us >= HOLD_S * 1000000; HOLD_S = min(CONFIRM_HOLD_S, CT_HOLD_CAP_S) for UI-3 and for the UI-5 Retire *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6)*, and DELETE_HOLD_S = min(2 * CONFIRM_HOLD_S, HOLD_EFF_MAX_S) for the UI-5 Delete *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)*; release, cancel, chart_table, pointer exit or focus loss sets t_us = 0 in the same frame`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t_us` | int, microseconds of UI process time | 0 to the threshold | this layer (CT-43); **not** simulation time, runs while the kernel is `PAUSED` | the held time on the layer's own clock |
| `dt_us` | int, microseconds | 0 to unbounded | the render delta | the frame delta before clamping |
| `UI_DT_MAX_MS` | int, ms | 33-100, default 50 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | this GDD | the per-frame delta clamp (CT-43) |
| `CONFIRM_HOLD_S` | float, seconds | per the registry (Tuning Knobs, cited rows): 0.6-1.9 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* | Player Controller, registry constant (cited, not retuned) | the quit-without-saving hold duration (UI-3); the same constant that closes SL OQ-32; the effective quit / Retire hold is 0.6-1.5 s (capped at `CT_HOLD_CAP_S`) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6)* |
| `DELETE_HOLD_S` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)* | float, seconds | `min(2 * CONFIRM_HOLD_S, HOLD_EFF_MAX_S)` (the Controller's effective-hold rule `H_v = min(m_v * base, HOLD_EFF_MAX_S)`, `m_v` 2, `HOLD_EFF_MAX_S` 1.9 s): **1.2-1.9** over the Controller's base range 0.6-1.9 (the cap binds from a base of 0.95 s), 1.6 at authoring | Player Controller (proposed name, derived, not yet registered; Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): D81) | the Delete hold duration (UI-5). Derived so that Delete is longer than the effective quit-without-saving / Retire hold by at least `DELETE_MARGIN_MIN_S` at every base (the quit / Retire hold is capped at `CT_HOLD_CAP_S`, 1.5 s) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6)* and so that an accessibility hold-duration option scales both together |
| `CT_HOLD_CAP_S` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6)* | float, seconds | 1.1-1.6, derived: `HOLD_EFF_MAX_S - DELETE_MARGIN_MIN_S` = 1.9 - 0.4 = 1.5 s | this GDD (derived, declared to the Controller as `cap_v`, PCI D.10) | the ceiling on the effective quit-without-saving and Retire holds so Delete stays at least `DELETE_MARGIN_MIN_S` longer at every accessible base |
| `HOLD_S` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | float, seconds | per action | calculated | the threshold of the action being held; the one place the gesture reads its duration |
| `L_BAR_HUD` | int, HUD px | 32-96, default 64 `[PLACEHOLDER]`; capped at `P_w div H - 2 * safe_x` | this GDD | the bar length; the bar sits on a full-bleed message plate (`UI3_PLATE`, UI-5), not inside the 71-px panel |
| `fill`, `needle_px` | float 0-1, int HUD px | 0-1, 0 to `L_BAR_HUD - 1` | calculated | the linear bar and its needle-mark (ART 7.3: linear, never radial) |

**Output Range:** `fill` in [0, 1]; the action fires at most once per gesture; a new gesture needs a new press edge (CT-22, CT-41).

**Example** (using the registry value of `CONFIRM_HOLD_S` at authoring, 0.8 s, so the threshold is 800,000 microseconds; the number is illustrative and the constant is the Controller's). At 60 fps, `dt_us = 16,667`. Frame 24: `t_us = 400,008`, `fill = 0.500`, `needle_px = floor(0.5 * 64) = 32`. Frame 47: `t_us = 783,349`, not yet. Frame 48: `t_us = 800,016 >= 800,000`, **fires**. Release at frame 30: `t_us = 0`, no action. Hitch: one 400 ms frame is clamped to 50 ms, so `fill` rises by 0.0625 and a stall can never complete a hold. At 10 fps (`dt_us = 100,000` clamped to 50,000) the bar advances at half real time and the hold takes 1.6 s: accepted, because a layer below 20 fps is already a failed frame budget. At either end of the Controller's range the same arithmetic holds with the threshold replaced. **Example, Delete (UI-5)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: `HOLD_S` = 1.6 s, so the threshold is 1,600,000 microseconds. At 60 fps (`dt_us` 16,667): frame 48 `t_us` = 800,016, `fill` = 0.500, `needle_px` = 32; frame 95 `t_us` = 1,583,365, not yet; frame 96 `t_us` = 1,600,032, **fires**. At 30 fps (`dt_us` 33,333): frame 48 = 1,599,984, not yet; frame 49 fires. At 10 fps (`dt_us` clamped to 50,000): frame 32, i.e. **3.2 s of wall time** (the 1.6 s above is the quit hold's 16 frames at 100 ms each; the two 1.6 figures are different quantities). Over the Controller's range, `CONFIRM_HOLD_S` 0.6 s gives a Delete hold of 1.2 s (frame 72 at 60 fps); a base of 0.95 s or more gives the capped 1.9 s (frame 114), so 1.2 s no longer gives 2.4 s (`H_v = min(m_v * base, HOLD_EFF_MAX_S)`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)*. Constraint satisfied: `DELETE_HOLD_S` exceeds the effective quit / Retire hold by at least 0.4 s at every base (at a base of 1.9 s: 1.9 s against 1.5 s) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6)*. **Delete-over-quit margin** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the cap collapses the margin exactly for the players who raised their hold duration for accessibility (the Alpha option), so this layer requires `DELETE_HOLD_S - HOLD_S(quit, Retire) >= DELETE_MARGIN_MIN_S` (0.4 s `[PLACEHOLDER]`, constraint C-10): the Controller caps the effective quit / Retire hold at `CT_HOLD_CAP_S` = 1.5 s (ASK-CT-19 answered), so the margin is 0.6 s at base 0.6, 0.8 at 0.8, 0.95 at 0.95, 0.7 at 1.2 and 0.4 s at every base from 1.5 to 1.9 (1,900,000 - 1,500,000 = 400,000 us >= 400,000); at base 1.9 and 60 fps the quit / Retire hold fires on frame 90 (1,500,030 us; frame 89 = 1,483,363) and Delete on frame 114 (1,900,038; frame 113 = 1,883,371); the authoring values (0.8 s and 1.6 s) satisfy it (margin 0.8 s), and no ruled value changes *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6)*.

### D.3 Snap-to-node

The snap-to-node formula is defined as:

`d = (x_c - x_f, y_f - y_c); phi = atan2(d.y, d.x) in degrees; delta = ((phi - theta + 540) mod 360) - 180 in [-180, 180); S1 = { c in F : -SNAP_SECTOR_DEG/2 <= delta(c) < +SNAP_SECTOR_DEG/2 }; S2 = { c in F : |delta(c)| < 90 }; chosen = argmin over (dist(f, c), |delta(c)|, ordinal(c)) lexicographically, over S1 if S1 is non-empty, else over S2 if S2 is non-empty, else the focus stays`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `f` | node | the focused node | this layer | where focus is now |
| `F` | set of nodes | the focusable nodes except `f` | D.4 plus HOME (CT-11 table) | the candidates; ORPHAN, berthed and absorbed records are not in it |
| `(x, y)` | int, frame art px, screen y down | frame-local | `chart_layout.json` (CT-12) | authored positions; `d.y` flips so that up is positive |
| `theta` | deg | 0 (right), 90 (up), 180 (left), 270 (down) | the pressed direction | cardinal navigation (CT-5); a `NAV_DOWN{dir}` event from the Adapter's four-way read (CT-45) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `SNAP_SECTOR_DEG` | float, deg | 60-120, default 90 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | this GDD | the width of the primary sector |
| `dist` | float, layout px | >= 0 | calculated | used **only for ordering and never displayed** (WM:1049, ASK-AD-7) |
| `ordinal(c)` | int | 1..N | `chart_layout.json` | stable authored ordinal, the last tie-break |

**Output Range:** a node in `F` or "stay"; no wrap, no shake, no flash. The half-open interval `[-half, +half)` puts a node exactly on a diagonal in **exactly one** sector (45 degrees belongs to up, not right; 315 degrees belongs to right, not down), so the result is deterministic. `delta` is rounded to four decimals before the comparison so a floating-point 44.99999999 does not decide a diagonal. Content-lint invariant: the snap graph over the authored layout is strongly connected (every focusable node reachable from every other in at most `N - 1` presses).

**Example** (illustrative data, not authored content; screen coordinates, y down): HOME (144, 100), A (210, 60), B (80, 40), `SNAP_SECTOR_DEG = 90`. Focus HOME, press right (theta 0): A has d = (66, 40), phi = 31.2, delta = 31.2, in `S1`; B has d = (-64, 60), phi = 136.9, delta = 136.9, out; result **A**. Press up (theta 90): A delta = -58.8, B delta = +46.9, both outside [-45, 45), so `S1` is empty; `S2` admits both (|delta| < 90); distances HOME-A 77.2 and HOME-B 87.7; result **A** (this is why the fallback exists: with three authored points, up from HOME would otherwise be a dead control). Press left (theta 180): B delta = -43.1, in `S1`; result **B**. Press down (theta 270): A delta = 121.2, B delta = -133.1, both outside 90, `S2` is empty: **focus stays** (no wrap). From A, press left: HOME delta = 31.2, result HOME. Stick navigation is edge-triggered at the Controller's deadzone with the repeat schedule of D.9.

### D.4 Legal-target mirror

The legal-target mirror formula is defined as:

`legal_targets(view) = {} if view.transit_state != IDLE or view.pod_at == IN_FLIGHT or (view.pod_at == HOME_ID and any(n.berthed and n.status != ABSORBED for n in view.nodes)) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; { n.derelict_id : n in view.nodes, n.kind == DERELICT, n.status not in {ABSORBED, ORPHAN}, not n.berthed } if view.pod_at == HOME_ID; { HOME_ID } if view.pod_at is a derelict id`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `view` | `web.view` | read-only | Web Map & Transit WM-36 (provisional name) | the only input; no other read |
| `view.nodes[].kind` | enum | HOME, DERELICT | WM-3 | the roster kind |
| `view.nodes[].status` | enum | UNSEEN, SEEN, BOARDED, ABSORBED, ORPHAN | WM-3 | the record status |
| `view.nodes[].berthed` | bool | | WM-36 | derived by WM |
| `view.pod_at` | record id or `IN_FLIGHT` | | WM-36 | where the pod is |
| `view.transit_state` | enum | IDLE, LEG, ARRIVING, BERTHING | WM-36 | the machine state |
| `HOME_ID` | `derelict_id` | | derived: the `derelict_id` of the `nodes[]` entry whose `kind` is HOME | a derived label, never a constant, never registered |

**Output Range:** a set of 0 to N - 1 record ids (the HOME singleton from a derelict). It is WM-4 evaluated for **selectability only**; it is not a validation and not a second copy of WM-9's reason order. **The mirror never reflects `FUEL_LOW`, `BUSY`, `OUT_OF_RANGE`, `NOT_LIT`, `ARRIVAL_BLOCKED` or `CAPACITY`** (CT-9): none of them is a `web.view` field *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: `BERTH_PENDING` is no longer in this list; the `berthed` field is read, CT-9)*, and greying a node on low fuel would put a fuel meter on the truth layer. A pure function of one `web.view` snapshot: a test double feeds two views that differ only in fuel and expects identical output (AC-CT-24).

**Example.** Pod at HOME, `transit_state` IDLE, A UNSEEN, B BOARDED and not berthed: `{A, B}` (UNSEEN is selectable, bare; WM:1299). B `berthed` and not ABSORBED: `{}` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; was `{A}`)* (the chart offers no choice that Web Map's E-36 would refuse; the console answers a POD press in Web Map's `BERTH_PENDING` words, CT-10 R6, OQ-CT-3). B ABSORBED and A ORPHAN: `{}`. Pod at A: `{HOME_ID}`. `pod_at == IN_FLIGHT`, or `transit_state == LEG`: `{}`. The roster {A SEEN, B UNSEEN, C ABSORBED, D SEEN and berthed} with the pod at HOME gives `{}` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; was `{A, B}`)*; the same roster with D welded in (ABSORBED) gives `{A, B}`.

### D.5 Filament drift

The filament drift formula is defined as:

`r = FILAMENT_DRIFT_FRACTION * V_DRIFT_CEIL; mean_us = round(1000000 * FILAMENT_DRIFT_STEP_PX / r); interval_us(i) = max(STEP_MIN_GAP_S * 1000000, (DRIFT_STEP_INTERVAL_PM[i mod 7] * mean_us) div 1000), with STEP_MIN_GAP_S = FILAMENT_DRIFT_STEP_PX / V_DRIFT_CEIL; each UI frame while r_eff > 0: acc_us += min(dt_us, UI_DT_MAX_MS * 1000); if acc_us >= interval_us(i) then one step is applied (n_applied += 1; i += 1; acc_us := 0), at most one applied step per frame; under reduced motion r_eff = 0 and acc_us, i and n_applied hold; offset_px = FILAMENT_DRIFT_STEP_PX * n_applied mod P_f; the offset is applied to the ring-1 filament sheet only` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, G2: creative-director lean, authored irregular step table)* (replaces the three step generators, the gap filter and its pending queue: the mechanism was over-engineered for a 0.5 px/s background that only has to never read as a pulse; the ceiling now holds by construction because every applied interval is floored at `STEP_MIN_GAP_S`)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `V_DRIFT_CEIL` | float, art px per second | the art-bible bound | ART 7.3 and 2.11 (cited, not restated with a value, not registered under a new name) | the ceiling "at most 1 art px per second, current ring only" |
| `FILAMENT_DRIFT_FRACTION` | float | 0.25-1.0, default 0.5 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | this GDD | the fraction of the ceiling actually used *(orchestrator ruling: the ux-designer's fraction knob over the ui-programmer's raw rate, because a raw number would be a second owner of an art-bible value, rulings R28; default 0.5 over the ux-designer's 1.0 so the layer sits visibly below the ceiling and "very low energy" holds, ART 2.11)* |
| `FILAMENT_DRIFT_STEP_PX` | int, art px | 1-2, default 1 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | this GDD | the quantum, so the texture never sits on a half pixel |
| `acc_us`, `i` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | int microseconds, int | 0 up; persist across opens | this layer (CT-43) | the step timer and the table index; they hold under reduced motion and do not rewind on re-open (they replace the three accumulators `a_k`) |
| `DRIFT_STEP_INTERVAL_PM` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, G2: creative-director lean, authored irregular step table)* | int per mille of the mean interval, 7 values summing to 7000 | defaults {1400, 600, 1150, 800, 1250, 700, 1100} `[PLACEHOLDER]` (the art-director signs the feel); cyclically adjacent entries differ by at least 250; max / min at least 2 | this GDD | the authored irregular step table, cycled in order; replaces `DRIFT_GEN_WEIGHT_PM` and `DRIFT_GEN_PHASE_PM`. The mean interval is preserved (the entries average 1000 per mille), so the mean rate is `r` whenever no entry is floored. At the defaults the stream repeats every 7 steps (14 s), which is accepted: the only player-facing requirement is that it never reads as a pulse, and no two neighbouring intervals are alike |
| `STEP_MIN_GAP_S` | float, seconds | derived: `FILAMENT_DRIFT_STEP_PX / V_DRIFT_CEIL` (1.0 s at the defaults) | this GDD from the cited ceiling | the floor of every applied interval; at a fraction of 1.0 the result is **at most one applied step per second** (a floored entry makes the mean rate slightly below 1 px/s), so the art-bible ceiling holds even instantaneously, not only on average *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `n_applied` | int | 0 up | calculated | the count of applied steps; `offset_px` is `FILAMENT_DRIFT_STEP_PX * n_applied` modulo `P_f` |
| `dt_us` | int, microseconds | | render delta | clamped by `UI_DT_MAX_MS` before it is added to `acc_us` |
| `P_f` | int, art px | content-defined, longer than the largest canvas plus one wrap period | art-director (asset A6) | the repeat length of the ring-1 sheet |

**Output Range** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, G2: creative-director lean, authored irregular step table)*: `offset_px` in `[0, P_f)`, whole art px; the **mean** step rate is `r / FILAMENT_DRIFT_STEP_PX` (0.5 per second at the defaults) and the applied intervals follow the authored table (the stream repeats every 7 steps, 14 s at the defaults; it is **not** aperiodic and the earlier claim is withdrawn). Acceptance (AC-CT-123): over 1,200 s of UI time at 60 fps (72,000 frames of `dt_us` 16,667) exactly **599** steps are applied, at frames 168, 240, 378, 474, 624, 708 and 840 of each 840-frame cycle, no two are closer than 60 frames (1 s), and no frame carries two applied steps; the interval statistics of the table (coefficient of variation 0.28, the largest 250-ms bin) are reported as **ADVISORY** observables only. Reduced motion holds `acc_us`, `i` and `n_applied` (no snap-back, no animation of the stop). Inputs are the UI clock and the reduced-motion toggle only (CT-40). **One table drives one sheet**: three overlapping sheets at different speeds would be parallax, which ART 7.3 bans between the ring plate and the filaments.

**Example** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; re-stated in the re-review as frame indices at 60 fps)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, G2: creative-director lean, authored irregular step table)*. Defaults (`FILAMENT_DRIFT_FRACTION` 0.5 of a ceiling of 1 art px/s, so `r = 0.5`), step 1, `mean_us` = 2,000,000, table {1400, 600, 1150, 800, 1250, 700, 1100}, so the applied intervals are 2,800, 1,200, 2,300, 1,600, 2,500, 1,400 and 2,200 ms (the smallest, 1,200 ms, is above the 1,000 ms floor) and `P_f = 720` (illustrative). At `dt_us` 16,667 the intervals take 168, 72, 138, 96, 150, 84 and 132 frames (`168 * 16,667 = 2,800,056 >= 2,800,000` while `167 * 16,667 = 2,783,389` is short), so steps land at frames **168, 240, 378, 474, 624, 708 and 840**, and `offset_px` is 0 through frame 167, 1 from frame 168, 2 from frame 240, 3 from frame 378, 4 from 474, 5 from 624, 6 from 708 and 7 from 840, then the cycle repeats. Over 72,000 frames: 85 whole cycles (595 steps, 71,400 frames) plus 600 frames (steps at 168, 240, 378 and 474) = **599** steps. At fraction 1.0: `mean_us` = 1,000,000 and the intervals are 1,400, 1,000 (the 600 entry is floored), 1,150, 1,000 (floored), 1,250, 1,000 (floored) and 1,100 ms, so no two steps are less than 60 frames apart and any rolling 1-s window holds at most one applied step. Reduced motion switched on at 100 s holds the timer, the index and the offset for as long as the toggle stays on; switched off it resumes from the held `acc_us`. A 400 ms hitch frame is clamped to 50 ms and adds 50 ms (2.5 % of the 2,000 ms mean interval) to `acc_us`, not 400 ms, and applies at most one step.

### D.6 Depth-ring ground steps

The depth-ring tone formula is defined as:

`tone(i, c) = G(c) - ((2 * (G(c) - V(c)) * i + CHART_RING_STEPS) div (2 * CHART_RING_STEPS)) for i in 0 .. CHART_RING_STEPS and each channel c; ring i + 1 is band i for i < CHART_RING_STEPS; the centre disc is i = CHART_RING_STEPS; ordering contract: for every i and c, tone(i, c) - tone(i + 1, c) >= RING_STEP_MIN_CHANNEL`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `G(c)` | int, 8-bit channel | (28, 33, 40) = Ground `#1C2128` | ART 4.1, 4.6 (cited) | ring 1, where every MVP node sits |
| `V(c)` | int, 8-bit channel | (11, 16, 24) = Void Steel `#0B1018` | ART 4.1, 4.6 (cited) | the centre disc, exactly |
| `CHART_RING_STEPS` | int | 1-5 by the contract (the span of 16 units allows `floor(16 / 3) = 5`); default 3, MVP content ceiling 3 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | this GDD | the number of ring bands drawn |
| `RING_STEP_MIN_CHANNEL` | int, 8-bit units | 2-6, default 3 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | this GDD (art-director ruling 5.1, checkpoint ruling Q5) | the minimum per-channel step so adjacent steps survive 8-bit quantisation and a poor panel |

**Output Range:** each tone lies between Ground and Void Steel and the sequence is strictly darker inward; the disc equals Void Steel exactly. The formula is the **ordering contract made concrete**: it restates no percentage (ART 7.4 and WM:1050 say "about 4 %", which cannot be satisfied together with "reaches Void Steel"; conflict row C16, routed to the art-bible owner). The tones are derived values, never new palette entries and never registered (R16, R28).

**Example** (`CHART_RING_STEPS = 3`, Ground (28, 33, 40), Void Steel (11, 16, 24)): ring 1 (i = 0) = (28, 33, 40) `#1C2128`; ring 2 (i = 1) = (22, 27, 35) `#161B23`; ring 3 (i = 2) = (17, 22, 29) `#11161D`; centre disc (i = 3) = (11, 16, 24) `#0B1018`. Per-channel steps are 6/6/5, 5/5/6 and 6/6/5, minimum 5 >= `RING_STEP_MIN_CHANNEL` 3, so the contract holds with margin. The art-director's illustrative tones (24, 29, 36), (20, 25, 32), (15, 20, 28) in the art-director's proposal are one evenly-divided reading over four intervals; the checkpoint ruling (Q5) fixed ring 1 = Ground exactly, so this interpolation governs and the exact on-screen values are verified in the spike (banding check by pixel sampling, AC-CT-47).

### D.7 Newest-record line

The newest-record line formula (main-menu Continue line only; read on every `MAIN_MENU` entry (re-read after a FAULTED return, a failed `new_game` or a Display & Comfort return, TX-34, TX-37; never refreshed while the menu stays open) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; the pause panel carries no save fact, CT-17) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* is defined as:

`HH = playtime div 3600; MM = (playtime mod 3600) div 60; SS = playtime mod 60; text = zero-pad(HH, 2) + ":" + zero-pad(MM, 2) + ":" + zero-pad(SS, 2)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `playtime` | int, seconds | >= 0 | Save/Load `SaveInfo.playtime` (`T div TICK_RATE`, whole seconds; read as given, never recomputed here) | the playtime of the newest generation, read on every `MAIN_MENU` entry (re-read after a FAULTED return, a failed `new_game` or a Display & Comfort return, TX-34, TX-37; never refreshed while the menu stays open) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* for the main-menu Continue line *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** `HH` grows past two digits without wrapping ("100:00:00" for 360,000 s); the string is set in the instrument face with tabular numerals; it is static while the Continue screen is open (no tick, no "time since"); no wall clock enters. **Width term** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `W_line = n_chars * ADV_INST_HUD`, with `ADV_INST_HUD` = 6 HUD px for the 7-px-cap face and 4 for the 5-px-cap face (`[UNV]`, estimates until the bitmap faces exist; `n_chars` = digits plus two colons, 8 for under 100 h, 9 for 100 to 999 h, 10 beyond). The 7-px face is used when `W_line <= content_w` of the host column; otherwise the 5-px face is used, and the lint asserts `10 * 4 = 40 <= content_w` at every row (56 at rows 4 and 6, so a 1,000-hour record still fits at the smallest canvas); a string is never truncated or wrapped.

**Example.** `playtime = 9641`: `HH = 2`, `MM = (9641 mod 3600) div 60 = 2441 div 60 = 40`, `SS = 41`, text `02:40:41`. `playtime = 0` gives `00:00:00`. `playtime = 59` gives `00:00:59`.

### D.8 Label fit

The label fit formula is defined as:

`W_text(key, row) = ceil(n_chars(key) * ADV_EM * em_px * TEXT_EXPANSION_TEST); a LABEL_1 key passes iff W_text <= content_w(row) * H(row); a LABEL_2 key passes iff the greedy word-wrap of its words at W_line = content_w(row) * H(row) gives at most two lines (a word is placed on the current line while the running width plus one space advance plus the word's width is at most W_line, else it starts a new line; a single word wider than W_line fails); a key that would need three lines fails` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (the earlier total-width test `W_text <= 2 * W_line` passed keys that cannot wrap, for example three words of 200 screen px at a 336 px line: total 626 <= 672, but the greedy wrap gives three lines)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `n_chars` | int | 1-80 | the `ct.*` key's string | the rendered character count of the whole key |
| `ADV_EM` | float | 0.5 (estimated) | **NOT SOURCEABLE** until the real reading face is imported | the average glyph advance as a fraction of the font size; the lint replaces `n_chars * ADV_EM * em_px` with the measured width of the real face at each row |
| `em_px` | int, screen px | the ART 7.1 menu-body floor | ART 7.1 (cited, 1080p and 1440p only; sub-1080p floors are ASK-UP-2 / OQ-CT-13) | the font size of panel labels (reading face, CT-17) |
| `TEXT_EXPANSION_TEST` | float | 1.2-1.5, default 1.3 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | this GDD | the localisation growth factor the lint applies to every key |
| `content_w`, `H` | int | D.1 | D.1 | the width available and the HUD scale of the row |

**Output Range:** pass or fail per key per row; a failing `LABEL_1` fails the build (a label is never truncated or ellipsised at runtime, CT-28).

**Example** (estimated advance, flagged `[UNV]`). "Display & Comfort", 17 characters, at 20 px: `ceil(17 * 0.5 * 20 * 1.3) = 221 <= 336` (row 6 content width in screen px, registry `safe_margin` rounded up), so it fits one line at every row; "Save & Quit" gives 143. A 36-character label gives `468 > 336`: it fails `LABEL_1` at rows 3 to 6 (content widths 348 and 336 screen px) and passes `LABEL_2` only if its greedy wrap at 336 gives two lines (for two roughly equal halves of about 234 px it does) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. At 1440p (row 1 canvas, `H = 4`, `em_px` 26): `W_panel = 2560 div 12 = 213`, `content_w = 213 - 4 - 32 = 177` HUD px = 708 screen px, and "Display & Comfort" needs `ceil(17 * 0.5 * 26 * 1.3) = 288`.

### D.9 Directional-navigation repeat

The navigation repeat formula is defined as:

`n_moves(t_held) = 1 if t_held < UI_NAV_REPEAT_MS else 1 + floor((t_held - UI_NAV_REPEAT_MS) / UI_NAV_REPEAT_RATE_MS) + 1; a move past the last node in the pressed direction is a no-op (no wrap); a direction edge is taken only after the stick passes MOVE_DEADZONE and is released only below MOVE_DEADZONE_EXIT`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t_held` | int, ms of UI process time | >= 0 | this layer (CT-43) | how long the direction has been held since its edge |
| `UI_NAV_REPEAT_MS` | int, ms | 250-500, default 350 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | this GDD | the delay before auto-repeat starts |
| `UI_NAV_REPEAT_RATE_MS` | int, ms | 80-200, default 120 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | this GDD | the interval between repeats |
| `MOVE_DEADZONE`, `MOVE_DEADZONE_EXIT` | float | Controller values | PCI, not registered (cited) | the hysteresis that stops a stick on the boundary from chattering; **applied by the Adapter** before it publishes `NAV_DOWN` / `NAV_UP` (CT-45), not by this layer *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** `n_moves` >= 1 once the edge is taken; a key or d-pad press uses the same schedule. **One move per frame** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: a frame applies at most one move, and a repeat that is due but not yet applied is dropped, never queued, so a hitch frame cannot move focus twice; the clamp `UI_DT_MAX_MS` (D.2) is constrained to `UI_DT_MAX_MS <= UI_NAV_REPEAT_RATE_MS` (C-9) so one clamped frame can make at most one repeat due.

**Example.** Held for 1,000 ms with 350 / 120: moves at 0, 350, 470, 590, 710, 830, 950 ms, so `n_moves = 1 + floor(650 / 120) + 1 = 1 + 5 + 1 = 7`. Held for 300 ms: 1. A 400 ms hitch frame during the hold is clamped to 50 ms and applies at most one move (not three) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

### D.10 Layer-3 draw-call estimate

The layer-3 draw-call estimate formula is defined as:

`D_L3 = (CHART_RING_STEPS + 1) + D_filament + 3 * N_nodes + D_pod + D_route + D_seam + D_frame + D_text + D_hints + D_bracket; D_L3 <= LAYER3_DRAW_CALL_BUDGET` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: each node now costs a Ground clearing, a glyph and at most a tick, 3 calls; layer 2 is hidden under a full-bleed plate)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `D_filament` | int | 3 | art-director assets A6 and A7 | the drifting sheet (ring 1; one sheet stepped by the authored interval table, D.5) plus the two static sheets |
| `D_L3` | int | about 20-35 on the chart *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | calculated | layer-3 draw calls while the chart is open |
| `CHART_RING_STEPS` | int | 1-5, default 3 | D.6, Tuning Knobs | ring bands; `+ 1` adds the centre disc |
| `N_nodes` | int | 3 at MVP | `web.view` | HOME plus two derelicts; each costs a Ground clearing, a glyph and, at most, a tick (3 calls); a berthed or absorbed record is a node drawn at its attach point (CT-11) |
| `D_pod`, `D_route`, `D_frame` | int | 0-1 each | CT-11 | pod marker, dashed route (one tiled quad), border and rivets |
| `D_seam` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | int | 0-2 | CT-11 | the berth seam quad (0-1) plus one Ground erase quad per ABSORBED record on the chart (0-2 at MVP; the sum is at most 2) |
| `D_bracket` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | int | 1 on the spatial chart (a focused node always exists), 0 otherwise | CT-6 | the bracket and bezel thickening, one draw |
| `D_text`, `D_hints` | int | 0-1 / 0-8 | CT-15, CT-28 | the `IN TRANSIT` word; control hints, **two draws per hint (one keycap glyph, one label run)** for 0-4 hints, so up to 8 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `LAYER3_DRAW_CALL_BUDGET` | int | default 40 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 30-60, and at least `D_L3` at the configured `CHART_RING_STEPS` and `N_nodes` (refused at load as `CONTENT_INVARIANT`, Tuning Knobs coupled constraint C-5) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix)* | this GDD; the Pipeline now carries the `D_L3` term in `D_root` (D.8 and D.16, ASK-CT-12 answered) | the cap *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: a number is set)* |

**Output Range** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; re-stated in the re-review for the bracket, the seam term and two-draw hints)*: about 20 to 35 calls on the chart (26 in the MVP example; at most 33 at `CHART_RING_STEPS` 5, three nodes, a seam and an erase quad, the `IN TRANSIT` word and the maximum four hints); fewer on the panel (a plate, four items, a bracket, a status slot). **Example:** 4 ring fills + 3 sheets + 9 node calls (clearing, glyph, tick for each of three nodes) + 1 pod + 1 route + 0 seam + 1 frame + 0 text + 6 hint draws (3 hints x 2) + 1 bracket = 26 <= 40 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (was 22; the bracket and the two-draw hints were missing). **Maximum:** 6 ring fills + 3 sheets + 9 + 1 + 1 + 2 seam + 1 + 1 text + 8 + 1 = 33 <= 40. This is an **estimate**: batching can lower it, and the Pipeline measures the real figure per viewport with `Viewport.get_render_info(RENDER_INFO_TYPE_CANVAS, ...)` (PIPE D.8, AC-CT-45). **Layer 2 under the open layer (OQ-CT-16 closed):** the HUD (layer 2) is **hidden at MVP while a full-bleed plate is open** (`CHART`, `CHART_VIEW_ONLY`, `UI3_PLATE`), because an opaque plate covers it entirely (CT-3) and the draws would be wasted; with the panel open it stays drawn, frozen and undimmed beside the panel (an active warning mark held ON, HS-23). `D_L3` therefore excludes `D_hud` on a full-bleed plate. In OVERLAY (VS-B) the HUD critical strip must stay above layer 3 (the ADR (b) constraint: the opaque panel must never cover the critical strip, only the hand rail, R-AD-6). The Pipeline now carries the layer-3 term (`D_root = D_hud + D_filter + D_hold + D_L3`, PIPE D.8 and D.16), which answers ASK-CT-12; the hiding of layer 2 is a request to the Pipeline owner (ASK-CT-12b).

### Values this GDD cites and does not own

Names and owners only; the numbers sit in the Tuning Knobs "cited" rows and nowhere in rule text (guard rail 5).

| Value | Owner | Used in |
|---|---|---|
| `CONFIRM_HOLD_S` | Player Controller (registry) | D.2, CT-22 |
| `H_default = max(k, 3)`, `HUD_MIN_SCALE`, `hud_multiplier` (registry); `cap_px = 7 * H` (PIPE D.4 variable, carried in `hud_multiplier`'s output, not a registry entry); the 5 % safe margin (registry `safe_margin`, source HUD & Suit Layer, rounded up per axis; PIPE:88, PR-2 Deck safe area, art bible 7.0 cite it) | Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline PR-12, D.4; HUD & Suit Layer (`safe_margin`) | D.1, D.1b, D.8 |
| fade band, bracket ease-out, drift ceiling | art bible 7.3 | CT-3, CT-13, D.5 |
| ring-step wording ("about 4 %", not restated), the right-third panel | art bible 7.4, WM:1050 | D.1, D.6, C16 |
| type floors, line length, glyph sizes, 3 Hz ceiling | art bible 7.1, 7.2, HR-13 | D.8, CT-28 |
| Ground, Void Steel and the rest of the UI palette | art bible 4.1, 4.6 | D.6 |
| `DEVICE_SWITCH_HYSTERESIS` | Player Controller (registry) | CT-6, D.3, D.9 |
| `MOVE_DEADZONE`, `MOVE_DEADZONE_EXIT` | Player Controller (not registered) | CT-6, D.3, D.9 |
| `SAVE_QUIT_TIMEOUT_MS` | Save/Load (3 000 ms default since 2026-10-28, range 2 000-15 000; registry request raised) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6)* | CT-21 |
| `HOP_FUEL_ML`, `BERTH_FUEL_ML`, `HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML`, `RETURN_HOP_FUEL_ML`, `HOP_LEG_TICKS`, `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` | Web Map & Transit (registry) | **named only in negative sentences; the layer displays none (CT-35)** |
| `TICK_RATE` | Command & Simulation Layer (registry) | D.7 (through `SaveInfo.playtime`) |

## Edge Cases

Format: **If [condition]**: [exact outcome]. [rationale]. Ids are `EC-CT-n` and are cited by the acceptance criteria. "SOLO" and "OVERLAY" are the two `PauseGate` modes (CT-4); a sentence that names neither holds in both. Where an outcome depends on a neighbour that has not decided, it is marked provisional and points at the ask or open question.

### A. The pick and the target

- **EC-CT-1. If the picked record becomes ABSORBED, ORPHAN or `berthed`, or disappears from `web.view`, while the chart is open**: `ChartPick := NONE` on that `web.view` change, the dashed line disappears in the same frame, the bracket stays where it is, and if the node holding key or pad focus left the focusable set, focus moves to HOME; no message is shown. A stale pick is the only way the chart and the console press can disagree (CT-8).
- **EC-CT-2. If a `request_restore` completes while any state of the layer is open (any `restore_reason`)**: the layer closes into `CLOSED` in the same frame with no animation (TX-27), `ChartPick := NONE`, and the machine is rebuilt from CLOSED. The restored world may not contain the record that was picked, and nothing about the layer is in a snapshot (CT-27).
- **EC-CT-3. If the pod console is pressed at HOME with no valid pick and exactly one legal target exists** (POD or DOCK prompt with nothing berthed, CT-10 R3 and R4b): the chart opens with focus on HOME; no hop, no refusal, no simulation effect (SOLO also requests the pause, CT-4, as a governed raise, CT-3). An auto-implied hop would make one press both the choice and the commitment (CT-10; WM:1225) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **EC-CT-4. If the pod console is pressed while the pod is at a derelict**: HOME is implied and the hop proceeds with no chart involvement; the pick is not read. If the chart is open instead, HOME is the only focusable node, the dashed line runs from the pod's node to HOME, accept on HOME does nothing and `ChartPick` stays NONE. The only legal move needs no choice (WM-4, WM-8; CT-10).
- **EC-CT-5. If accept lands on the already-picked node, on HOME while the pod is at HOME, or on a non-selectable node**: the picked node stays picked (idempotent); HOME clears the pick; a non-selectable node (ORPHAN, `berthed`) does nothing. A double press must never clear a pick by accident (CT-7).
- **EC-CT-6. If the picked record changes status on the same presentation frame as the console press**: the Adapter injects whatever the pick held when the press was processed, and Web Map & Transit's own reasons decide (`TARGET_ABSORBED`, `TARGET_BERTHED`, `TARGET_UNKNOWN`), shown as words on the register plate by Interaction. The chart never re-validates a command; it only mirrors selectability (CT-9).
- **EC-CT-7. If every derelict is ABSORBED or ORPHAN (the legal set is empty)**: the chart opens with HOME as the only focusable node, no message, no "all charted" line; a no-pick console press still opens the chart. There is no completion state and no win state (anti-pillar; WM guard rail 5).
- **EC-CT-8. If an unwelded derelict B is `berthed` and the player opens the chart** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: `legal_targets` is `{}` (CT-9), so no derelict can be picked (the earlier "A stays selectable" is retired); a stale pick made before the berthing is invalidated by CT-8 and its route removed; a POD press at the console is answered in Web Map's `BERTH_PENDING` words and opens no chart (CT-10 R6). The mirror reads only the `berthed` field and does not copy WM-9's reason order (CT-9; OQ-CT-3).
- **EC-CT-9. If a fuel level, a hop timer or any other Graph value changes while the chart is open**: selectability, focus and drawing are unchanged. The mirror never reads `FUEL_LOW`, `BUSY`, `OUT_OF_RANGE`, `NOT_LIT`, `ARRIVAL_BLOCKED` or `CAPACITY`; it reads the `berthed` field, and the empty set while a record is `berthed` and not ABSORBED is the whole of its `BERTH_PENDING` reflection (CT-9, D.4 Output Range) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G4)*; a fuel-dependent greying would be a fuel meter on the truth layer (CT-9, CT-35; AC-CT-24).
- **EC-CT-80. If a derelict B is berthed and the DOCK prompt is pressed while `ChartPick` holds A (or any value)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the press is `unberth{}` (CT-10 R5); the pick is not read, not injected and not cleared, and the chart does not open. Before this rule, rule 3 ("no valid pick opens the chart") could swallow an unberth press, which would have trapped a berthed-but-never-welded pod (WM-22).
- **EC-CT-81. If the chart was opened by a console press and the player presses `cancel`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the layer returns to `CLOSED` and SOLO resumes (TX-35), not to a `PANEL` the player never opened; `chart_table` also closes (TX-15). Opened from the panel, `cancel` returns to the panel (TX-19). The entry origin is host-local and is lost on any restore (TX-27).

### B. Pause, input and focus

- **EC-CT-10. If Esc (or Start) is held and key echo arrives**: only the first non-echo press toggles the layer; the Adapter suppresses echoes (CT-45) so the layer never sees them. A held Esc must not flicker the layer open and shut (CT-41(3); key-echo detection is NOT SOURCEABLE in the engine reference, ADR candidate (e)) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **EC-CT-11. If `chart_table`, `cancel`, release, pointer exit or window focus loss occurs during a hold-to-confirm fill**: the bar snaps to empty in the same frame and nothing else happens; a second `chart_table` press then closes the layer. One press must not abort a destructive gesture and also resume the simulation (CT-5, CT-22, TX-10).
- **EC-CT-12. If `chart_table` and `cancel` arrive on the same frame**: they are processed in that order, `chart_table` closes the whole layer, and the `cancel` is consumed with nothing to step back to. One frame, one effect (CT-5).
- **EC-CT-13. If `chart_table` is pressed twice inside one tick boundary (SOLO)**: the layer's own `open` boolean toggles twice and `PauseGate` re-issues the latest desired state until the kernel state matches, so the layer is never wedged half-open. Whether the kernel coalesces or executes a same-boundary `request_pause` and `request_resume` in order is ASK-CT-9; the layer needs only that it converges (CT-4).
- **EC-CT-14. If the last-used device switches mid-navigation**: glyphs swap after `DEVICE_SWITCH_HYSTERESIS`, key and pad focus is retained, the bracket hides on mouse motion; the next key or pad navigation press reveals it on the retained focus without moving it (CT-45 (c)), and the press after that moves focus (CT-6; PC-13) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Mixed input is permitted and must not lose the player's place.
- **EC-CT-15. If the mouse hovers item X while the pad focuses item Y**: X's label is Tungsten, the bracket is on Y, pad accept (`ACCEPT_DOWN`) activates Y, and a click (a `POINTER` DOWN and UP on X; **never an `ACCEPT`**) activates X and moves key and pad focus to X while the bracket stays hidden until the next key or pad input. Godot 4.6 split the two focus kinds, so both are tested (CT-6, CT-45; `modules/ui.md`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **EC-CT-16. If a click begins on one item and the button is released on another (or off all items)**: nothing activates and no fill remains. A click is an activation only when pointer-up lands on the item that received the press (CT-6).
- **EC-CT-17. If the window loses focus with the layer open (mid-hold, mid-navigation, or idle)**: any hold fill goes to empty, navigation state is cleared, the layer stays open with the previous focus on return, and the kernel state is unchanged (no auto-resume). Focus-out notifications are NOT SOURCEABLE in the engine reference (CT-41(5); ADR candidate (e)).
- **EC-CT-18. If the wheel, `=`, `-`, or d-pad up or down is pressed while the layer is open** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; replaces "a zoom press ... is discarded")*: the Controller emits zoom edges from `gameplay` and `inspect_card` only, so none reaches the layer. The d-pad up and down are **navigation** (`NAV_DOWN` with source DPAD, CT-45); the wheel scrolls only a focused scrolling control, and none exists at MVP; `=` and `-` do nothing here; no zoom hint or glyph is shown. Zoom is not an action of the `chart_table` context and never a setting (CT-5; PIPE:688, PIPE:232).
- **EC-CT-19. If the layer was opened by a console press and that press releases or auto-repeats**: nothing on the layer activates; every activation needs a press edge that begins after the open frame. Otherwise the LMB release that follows a console press could activate whatever is under the cursor (CT-41(1)).
- **EC-CT-20. If `interact` on Resume closes the layer while the player stands at the pod console**: the closing press and its release are consumed and gameplay sees no `interact` edge until a fresh press, so no `pod_hop` is submitted by the same finger. The next `interact` edge at the console is the irreversible act (CT-41(2); WM:1225).
- **EC-CT-21. If the stick rests on the deadzone boundary during chart navigation**: the Adapter's exit threshold stops chatter, one move is taken per direction edge, and repeats follow D.9; a held direction never wraps past the last node. A diagonal stick takes the dominant axis (a tie goes to vertical) and does not alternate between two directions. The four-way cardinal read and the hysteresis are the Adapter's (CT-45) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **EC-CT-22. If a gamepad disconnects while the layer is open**: the layer stays open, glyphs switch to the last remaining device (keyboard if none), and no pause or close is forced. A disconnect with the layer closed is the Controller's (PCI), not this layer's.
- **EC-CT-23. If the player clicks the frozen world outside the panel**: nothing happens. The world is a held frame, and a click there would read as an in-world action (CT-41(4)).

### C. Transit and the chart

- **EC-CT-24. If the chart opens during LEG or ARRIVING**: it opens as `CHART_VIEW_ONLY` with focus on Back; nothing is focusable or activatable; the `IN TRANSIT` word shows iff `pod_at == IN_FLIGHT`; on an OUT leg the route HOME to `web.view.leg_target_id` is drawn (never from the pick); on a BACK leg the route origin-to-HOME is drawn from `leg_origin_id` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G2)*; `web.leg_timing()` is never called. The chart never reconstructs a target from its own pick (CT-15).
- **EC-CT-25. If the chart opens during BERTHING**: no state word and no progress element are shown, the pick is frozen and nothing is drawn from it. A berth is witnessed by the world (WM:1307).
- **EC-CT-26. If the layer is opened during the Pipeline's `TRANSIT_HOLD` (layer-1 black)**: the panel appears over the black hold; in SOLO the pause freezes `T` as always (CR-16), so the leg does not advance while the panel is up; Resume returns to the hold. The pause is available and "changes nothing" the player can use (WM:1268; PIPE:264).
- **EC-CT-27. If `transit_state` leaves IDLE while the chart is open (OVERLAY only; in SOLO `T` is frozen)**: `CHART` becomes `CHART_VIEW_ONLY` (TX-28), focus moves to Back and the pick is frozen; on return to IDLE the pick has been reset by arrival and the state returns to `CHART` with focus on HOME (TX-29). No message in either direction.
- **EC-CT-28. If `web.view` carries `leg_target_id`, `leg_origin_id` and `leg_dir` in flight (WM-36)**: on an OUT leg the route HOME to `leg_target_id` is drawn only from them, never from the pick (CT-15); on a BACK leg the route runs from `leg_origin_id` to HOME; if the needed id reads NONE or names no node, no route is drawn and the `IN TRANSIT` word stands alone *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G2)*.

### D. Saving, quitting and failures

- **EC-CT-29. If Save & Quit is accepted while any bound seat is `DOWNED` or `DEAD`** (the `DEAD` case is unreachable in the solo MVP, SL-6): nothing is written, `UI3_PLATE` opens with Back as the default focus, the string states that the newest autosave will be used, and Quit without saving needs the full hold. This is Save/Load's gate (`SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED`); the layer adds no death language (CT-21; SL UI-3).
- **EC-CT-30. If Save & Quit is accepted in the first tick after opening, before the kernel confirms `PAUSED`**: the item is disabled (the Hull Grey disabled style of CT-VA-7) because `can_save_quit` is false until the kernel confirms, so no request is made. The fade (`CHART_FADE_MS`, inside the ART 7.3 band) hides the gap (CT-4).
- **EC-CT-31. If Save/Load publishes `FAILED` and `quit_held` after `SAVE_QUIT_TIMEOUT_MS`, or `save_status` reads `FAILED`** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6)*: the layer is the panel with the plain word `FAILED` in the panel's status-line position (CT-17); the four items stay in CT-3 order; Resume, Chart, Display & Comfort and `chart_table` work. The word is never colour alone and never Alarm Red (CT-21; HR-4; SL:785).
- **EC-CT-32. If Retry fails again**: the panel returns with `FAILED`, with no counter, no escalation text and no changed colour. Accepting Save & Quit opens `UI3_PLATE` each time, so Quit without saving stays one hold away. A save is not an instrument and the layer adds no affect (CT-21).
- **EC-CT-33. If `chart_table` or `cancel` is pressed while the write runs (`SAVE_QUIT_WAIT`)**: ignored; nothing is added to the screen (no spinner, ring, bar, percentage or text change). A half-written state is never abandoned, and SL H5 forbids a write indicator (CT-5, CT-21).
- **EC-CT-34. If Save/Load's `save_and_quit` finds the state hash unchanged** (`SL_QUIT_DEDUPED`, SL:784): it writes nothing and calls `request_shutdown` itself (SL-6); the layer shows nothing different and calls nothing. The layer therefore cannot tell a written quit from a skipped one, and must not try to (CT-21).
- **EC-CT-35. If the UI-3 hold completes**: the layer calls `request_shutdown` once with no write, and further input is ignored for the frames before the process ends. The newest autosave stands (CT-21; TX-26).
- **EC-CT-36. If the kernel enters `FAULTED` in a release build with the layer open**: the layer closes and the boot-flow main menu opens with Continue offered and focused; no pause is requested and no string is invented (OQ-CT-5). In `FAULTED` the kernel never autosaves over an existing save, so the newest verified generation is intact (CT-26; KRN:151).
- **EC-CT-37. If Quit is accepted on a boot-flow screen** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the layer calls `request_shutdown` once, exactly as on the FAULTED-return menu (TX-30). The kernel's CR-15, as amended 2026-10-27, accepts `request_shutdown` from every state except `SHUTTING_DOWN`, including `BOOTING` and `LOADING`, so no boot-code path without a kernel call is needed and ASK-CT-11's boot half is answered. No write occurs (no session exists).
- **EC-CT-82. If the player quits (Quit or Quit without saving) while a queued re-open is pending, or from a boot screen with a hold running** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the queue and any running fill are cleared before the call; the shutdown path is one call and further input is ignored (TX-26, TX-30).
- **EC-CT-38. If the operating system's window-close request arrives**: the layer calls nothing and adds nothing to the screen. With the layer closed (kernel `RUNNING`), Save/Load's own handler runs `save_and_quit()` from `RUNNING` and writes a `QUIT` generation, or dedupes (`SL_QUIT_DEDUPED`), then calls `request_shutdown` (SL-6, AC-SL-44); **while a seat is `DOWNED` it writes nothing and holds the close** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G1)*: Save/Load owns this behaviour (it holds the close for the hold-to-confirm "quit without saving", SL edge case on a window close while a seat is `DOWNED`) and the layer conforms, so the earlier silent shutdown for the closed-layer, `DOWNED` case is retired and the process does not exit; the layer is raised to `UI3_PLATE` from `CLOSED` (TX-38, `request_pause` on the same frame in SOLO), the plate's cancel returns to `CLOSED` with `request_resume`; while a window-close write is in progress the layer is locked in `SAVE_QUIT_WAIT` (TX-39; the lock follows `quit_write_active`) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6)*. With the layer open in SOLO (kernel `PAUSED`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: answered by Save/Load, SL-6 edge case "window closed while the kernel is `PAUSED` under the open Chart Table")*: Save/Load's handler runs `save_and_quit()` from `PAUSED` (`PAUSED -> SAVING -> PAUSED`) under SL-6's rules and then `request_shutdown`; the layer **stays open and calls nothing**, it is never turned into a resume (the earlier degraded branch is retired). If a seat is `DOWNED` the result is `SL_QUIT_NO_WRITE_DOWNED`, the process does not exit, and the layer shows `UI3_PLATE` (TX-38; from any state including `CLOSED`) so the player can choose Back or Quit without saving by hold. On a boot-flow screen nothing is written and the process ends through the Quit path (TX-30). The layer is never a second save path (SL-24).

### E. Boot, campaigns and settings

- **EC-CT-39. If Continue finds the newest generation corrupt**: Save/Load falls back to the one before it, and the UI-7 line "Newest record unreadable. Continuing from the one before it." (placeholder copy) is shown under the Continue entry iff `campaign_info()` reports the failed verification (`newest_failed_verification`, SL-21; ASK-CT-5 answered), otherwise it is the HUD & Suit Layer's on the first `RUNNING` frame. No repair is offered (CT-25).
- **EC-CT-40. If no readable record exists (`SL_LOAD_NO_LOADABLE_SAVE`)**: the boot-flow UI-8 plate shows New campaign (default focus) and Quit; no pause is requested; the layer re-enabled itself from `LOAD_HOLD`. Nothing is offered that would imply a save exists (CT-25; C7).
- **EC-CT-41. If the save is from an incompatible build**: the UI-9 plate shows the message with Quit and New campaign; nothing is overwritten or repaired, and New campaign starts a fresh campaign without touching the incompatible file (SL:752). The player's old record is kept for a build that can read it (CT-25).
- **EC-CT-42. If New campaign is chosen with an existing campaign** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: UI-5 opens with **Back** as the default focus; the press that opened it (and its release or repeat) activates nothing (CT-41(1) on every boot transition); Retire is a hold item (`CONFIRM_HOLD_S`) whose file stays on disk but which the game cannot bring back *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; Delete is a separate item under the longer hold gesture; `cancel` returns to the main menu with focus on New campaign. The destructive items look like every other item and are distinguished only by the bar (CT-24, CT-22) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. A double-tap on New campaign therefore ends on UI-5 with nothing retired.
- **EC-CT-43. If a Delete hold is released early**: the bar is empty, nothing is deleted, and a new gesture needs a new press edge (CT-22). A retired file stays on disk and there is no un-retire screen at MVP (OQ-CT-4) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "reversible" is no longer claimed)*.
- **EC-CT-44. If `continue_campaign()` or `new_game(world_seed)` returns a failure after the black frame** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: a `new_game` failure now has a visible line; OQ-CT-17 closed)*: `continue_campaign()` failures re-enable the layer on UI-8 or UI-9 with Save/Load's plain message (TX-11); a `new_game` failure (or any other failure) re-enables it on the main menu with one plain ship-log line under New campaign (TX-34; placeholder "Could not start a new campaign."), so a silent failure is never the requirement. Nothing is drawn between the black frame and the first `RUNNING` frame otherwise (CT-42, TX-33).
- **EC-CT-45. If the first launch is repeated (the settings store lost or unreadable)**: the photosensitivity notice appears again, identical, with reduce flashing ON (the first-launch default) and reduced motion at the pipeline default, and master volume at its top step; nothing else differs *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Showing it again is the safe direction (CT-19, CT-20).
- **EC-CT-83. If master volume is set to step 0 (muted)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* (Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20), D85): the page shows the numeral 0 and nothing else changes: no warning, no prompt, no confirmation, no colour. The step is stored, published and applied to the first audio frame of the next boot (the store is read before the main menu, so a muted player is never greeted by a cue at the old level). Every piece of information the game carries only in sound has its own non-audio channel or accessibility option (Adaptive Audio and the HUD own them); this layer adds none.
- **EC-CT-84. If the Display & Comfort page is shown at a canvas with `m < 1` (rows 4 and 6, the forced-`H` hook or the Alpha HUD scale)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: the six-row page shrinks its item height per D.1c (`ITEM_H_S6` 15 at row 4, 13 at row 6) so it still fits without scrolling; no key may wrap there (`n_wrap_S6 = 0`, the fit-class lint treats the page's keys as `LABEL_1`); a configuration whose gap and back-hint heights leave no room even at the 12-px item floor is refused at load by C-3.
